# Djinn UX Findings

Append-only log of UX issues discovered by `/djinn-e2e`. The `/djinn` main loop picks up the highest-severity open items (unchecked boxes) each iteration and fixes them.

**Rules:**
- `/djinn-e2e` APPENDS new findings at the top. Never edits existing entries.
- `/djinn` (or anyone fixing) marks items with `[x] (fixed in <commit-sha>) ...` when resolved. Never deletes findings — the trail matters.
- Severity rubric:
  - **CRITICAL**: blocks the golden path
  - **HIGH**: user gets stuck or churns
  - **MED**: noticeable friction
  - **LOW**: polish

---

### 2026-05-08 — MED — Horizontal overflow on /genius page at 390px mobile: bare hex USDC address in step-3 hint has no break-all

- [x] (fixed) Added `break-all` to the onboarding hint `<p>` at `web/app/genius/page.tsx:287` so the bare USDC hex string can wrap mid-character on narrow viewports. Single-line change, follows the triage hint exactly.
- [ ] Reported by `/djinn-e2e` heuristic (iter-100, 2026-05-08).
- **Symptom:** On the unauthenticated `/genius` marketing page at 390x844 (iPhone 13), `document.body.scrollWidth > document.body.clientWidth` is true -- a visible horizontal scrollbar appears at the bottom of the page. First-time mobile visitors who land on `/genius` before connecting a wallet see a broken layout.
- **Diagnosis:** Commit `3b88cbf4` (2026-05-01, "Fix 2026-05-01 genius USDC onboarding") introduced `web/lib/geniusOnboarding.ts` with the step 3 hint: `"...Token: ${usdcAddress}..."`. The USDC address `0x00e8293b05dbD3732EF3396ad1483E87e7265054` is a 42-character unbreakable string. It is rendered in `<p className="text-xs text-slate-500 mt-0.5">{hint}</p>` at `web/app/genius/page.tsx:307`. With default `word-break: normal`, the browser cannot split the hex string, so the flex text container expands to the full address width (~294px in sans-serif at 12px), which marginally exceeds the available mobile column width. The commit explicitly noted "UI not browser-verified (WAF block)" -- this is the regression that slipped through.
- **Repro:** `cd ~/djinn/web && xvfb-run npx playwright test --config playwright.ux.config.ts e2e/ux/journeys/heuristic-sweep.spec.ts 2>&1 | grep -A1 "Mobile findings"` -- should report the overflow. Alternatively: `await page.setViewportSize({width:390,height:844}); await page.goto("https://djinn.gg/genius"); return document.body.scrollWidth > document.body.clientWidth` in a Playwright `evaluate()`.
- **Triage hint:** One-line fix in `web/app/genius/page.tsx` at the hint `<p>` inside the `getGeniusOnboardingSteps().map(...)` block: add `break-all` to the className. Alternatively, truncate the address in `geniusOnboarding.ts` using `${usdcAddress.slice(0,6)}...${usdcAddress.slice(-4)}` since the address is not useful to copy from a step hint. Do NOT add `overflow-x: hidden` to the body -- that masks the symptom and hides future overflows.
- **Screenshot:** `e2e-screenshots/2026-04-22-heuristic-mobile-genius.png`

### 2026-05-08 — HIGH — Vercel Code 99 blocks MCP heuristic sweep: new challenge code persists >5 minutes, never self-resolved

- [ ] Reported by `/djinn-e2e` heuristic (iter-99, 2026-05-08).
- **Symptom:** MCP browser (fresh session, no stored cookies) hits `https://djinn.gg` and receives Vercel Security Checkpoint Code 99 ("Failed to verify your browser"). After >5 minutes of waiting and multiple navigation attempts including `www.djinn.gg`, the checkpoint never resolved. The site was completely unreachable for the heuristic sweep. The automated regression suite (which carries a stored session cookie from prior runs) auto-resolved the checkpoint normally during visitor/resilience journeys.
- **Diagnosis:** Code 99 is a new error code not seen in prior iters (all prior runs showed Code 29 which typically auto-resolved in 10-20s). Code 99 may indicate a stricter fingerprinting challenge that requires JavaScript proof-of-work or browser telemetry not present in a headless Playwright context without session history. The session-cookie-carrying regression test instance resolved normally, confirming the site is up -- this is a fingerprinting/bot-detection escalation, not an outage. Vercel Attack Mode toggle at `https://vercel.com/djinn-inc/djinn/security` may have been tightened.
- **Repro:** Open a fresh Playwright browser (no stored cookies), navigate to `https://djinn.gg`, wait 5 minutes. Code 99 persists. Session-cookie-bearing instance resolves in ~30s.
- **Triage hint:** Check if Vercel Attack Mode was manually escalated since iter-98. If not, Vercel may have automatically escalated based on traffic patterns. The fix is either (a) disable Attack Mode, (b) add a static passthrough rule for known test IPs, or (c) accept that heuristic sweeps require a pre-warmed session cookie (current regression suite approach works). The heuristic sweep was unable to complete; observations below are from the regression suite output only.
- **Screenshot:** e2e-screenshots/ux-a11y-vercel-code99-2026-05-08.png

### 2026-04-30 — MED — on-chain-health spec fails when sepolia.base.org returns 502; no actual proxy-owner drift

- [x] (fixed in 1fc8e83d, v1627) Reported by `/djinn-e2e` regression (on-chain-health.spec.ts:69:7, iter-97). — Changed spec default RPC from `sepolia.base.org` to `base-sepolia-rpc.publicnode.com`.
- **Symptom:** `on-chain-health.spec.ts` fails with "proxy-owner drift: OutcomeVoting.owner() threw: Error: server response 502 Bad Gateway". The second test (bytecode check) is DNR due to serial cascade. Direct cast call via publicnode RPC confirms all 7 proxies are correctly owned by `0x37f41EFfa8492022afF48B9Ef725008963F14f79` (TimelockController). No actual ownership drift occurred.
- **Diagnosis:** The spec uses `https://sepolia.base.org` as the default RPC (via `ON_CHAIN_HEALTH_RPC` env var fallback). Base Sepolia's public RPC was returning 502 during this test run. The same RPC has been known to be unreliable (noted in `feedback_base_sepolia_rpc_logs_broken.md`). The spec should use `https://base-sepolia-rpc.publicnode.com` as its default.
- **Repro:** `xvfb-run npx playwright test --config playwright.ux.config.ts e2e/ux/journeys/on-chain-health.spec.ts` — when sepolia.base.org is degraded, test fails with 502 on any ethers.js provider.call().
- **Triage hint:** Fix is setting `ON_CHAIN_HEALTH_RPC=https://base-sepolia-rpc.publicnode.com` in `web/.env.local`, OR changing the spec default from `https://sepolia.base.org` to `https://base-sepolia-rpc.publicnode.com`. The latter is a one-line change in `web/e2e/ux/journeys/on-chain-health.spec.ts:9`. Do NOT assume proxy drift is real until cast call via publicnode also fails.

### 2026-04-24 20:30 UTC — HIGH — Vercel hard-403 blocking all page-navigation regression tests; zero self-healing this iter

- [ ] Reported by `/djinn-e2e` regression (visitor.spec.ts, heuristic-sweep.spec.ts, resilience.spec.ts, attest.spec.ts, idiot.spec.ts — all fail same root cause, iter-85).
- **Symptom:** Every test that calls `landOnSite()` to navigate djinn.gg fails with "Vercel hard-403 'Error: Forbidden' persisted after 2 cooldown+reload attempts (90s + 120s)". The test timeout (120s) is shorter than the total cooldown needed (90s + 120s = 210s), so all page-navigation tests time out. Parameterized API-read tests (node fetch, not browser) and on-chain-health tests (direct RPC, no browser nav) continue to pass. MCP browser also blocked with "Failed to verify your browser - Code 29". 15 tests DNR as serial cascade from first-test failure.
- **Diagnosis:** Dev-box IP is fingerprinted by Vercel Attack Mode. The saved session cookies (`_vcrcs`, age=0.2h, at `web/e2e/ux/.vercel-session.json`) are failing to suppress the challenge — the hard-403 fires before the JS challenge can run. In prior iters the checkpoint auto-resolved after 4 attempts; today it never resolved across 3 separate test batch runs (~90 min total). The `landOnSite()` cooldown windows (90s + 120s) exceed the per-test timeout (120s in this run), causing timeout rather than the expected hard-403 detection. Underlying cause: Vercel Attack Mode (confirmed ON via `project_vercel_attack_mode_2026_04_20.md`) has tightened fingerprinting of this runner's IP.
- **Repro:** `cd web && xvfb-run npx playwright test --config playwright.ux.config.ts e2e/ux/journeys/visitor.spec.ts --timeout=120000` — fails with 403: Forbidden after 2m.
- **Triage hint:** Three fixes in priority order: (1) Set `VERCEL_BYPASS_SECRET` in `web/e2e/ux/.env` — this is the most reliable fix; get the value from Vercel project Settings > Security > Automation Bypass Secret. (2) Raise per-test timeout back to 300s (5min) so the 210s cooldown can complete before timeout (works when 403 is transient). (3) Long-term: move e2e off Vercel entirely to the VPS nginx path. Fix (1) is a 2-minute config change with no code edits.
- **Screenshot:** e2e-screenshots/vercel-hard403-iter85-2026-04-24.png

### 2026-04-24 18:10 UTC — MED — heuristic-sweep /genius test times out on teardown; 7 heuristic tests silently absent every run

- [x] Fixed 2026-05-08 (commit 63d32b8a). `waitForIdle` in `web/e2e/ux/helpers/human.ts:78` now passes `{ timeout: 3_000 }` to `waitForLoadState("networkidle")` so background-polling pages (TanStack `refetchInterval`, SSE) fall through after 3s instead of burning the full 30s default. With ~3 calls per /genius test, that's a 90s reduction per test and removes the 300s teardown overrun.
- [ ] Reported by `/djinn-e2e` regression (heuristic-sweep.spec.ts:118, iter-84).
- **Symptom:** `heuristic-sweep.spec.ts` test 14 (`/genius page: jargon + clarity`) fails with "Tearing down context exceeded the test timeout of 300000ms" even though the /genius page renders correctly. Heuristic tests 15-21 (idiot, leaderboard, network, docs, about, mobile, summary) never run because `test.describe.configure({mode:"serial"})` aborts the block on test 14 failure. Net effect: 7 of 9 heuristic sub-tests are dark every run, providing no signal on those pages.
- **Diagnosis:** `waitForIdle` at `web/e2e/ux/helpers/human.ts:78` calls `page.waitForLoadState("networkidle")` which requires 500ms of zero network activity. The /genius page has background polling (likely via TanStack Query's `refetchInterval`) that issues periodic API requests, keeping the network permanently active. `networkidle` never fires, so the test hangs until the 300s global timeout, then teardown also times out.
- **Repro:** `cd web && xvfb-run npx playwright test --config playwright.ux.config.ts --grep "genius page"` — times out at 6m.
- **Triage hint:** Fix: replace `await page.waitForLoadState("networkidle")` in `waitForIdle` with `await page.waitForLoadState("domcontentloaded")` + a fixed 500ms pause, OR add a `page.route` intercept to quiet polling during heuristic tests. Since the heuristic spec can't edit product code, the helper is the right fix. Alternatively add `test.setTimeout(30_000)` per-test on the genius heuristic test and use `domcontentloaded`. One-line fix in `web/e2e/ux/helpers/human.ts:78`.
- **Screenshot:** e2e-screenshots/2026-04-24-vercel-checkpoint-iter84.png (Vercel checkpoint seen on MCP browser; Playwright test itself shows /genius page loaded correctly in test-results/heuristic-sweep-Heuristic--a3418--genius-page-jargon-clarity-ux-journeys/test-failed-1.png).

### 2026-04-23 22:15 UTC — LOW — Top nav "Attest" label inconsistent with footer "Verify Picks"; both link to /attest

- [x] Fixed 2026-05-08 (commit 63d32b8a). `web/components/Layout.tsx:18` `NAV_LINKS` now reads `{ href: "/attest", label: "Verify Picks" }` so desktop top nav and mobile hamburger match the footer label. e2e callers updated to `clickNav(page, "Verify Picks")`.
- [ ] Reported by `/djinn-e2e` heuristic (source scan, iter-77).
- **Symptom:** The primary top navigation bar renders "Attest" (`web/components/Layout.tsx:18` — `NAV_LINKS` entry). The footer navigation renders "Verify Picks" (`web/components/Layout.tsx:265`). Both link to `/attest`. A first-time bettor sees "Attest" in the sticky nav header on every page — a verb with no bettor-vocabulary equivalent — while the footer correctly uses plain English "Verify Picks". The mismatch also creates a UI consistency issue (two nav items pointing to the same page but with different labels).
- **Diagnosis:** The v1592 de-jargon fix (commit `0d57fe17`) renamed the footer's "Web Attestation" link to "Verify Picks" but did not update the `NAV_LINKS` array label. The `NAV_LINKS` label at line 18 feeds the desktop top nav AND the mobile hamburger menu. The footer link at line 265 is a separate `<Link>` element.
- **Repro:** Visit https://djinn.gg on desktop or mobile. Observe the sticky top nav — it reads "Attest". Scroll to footer — it reads "Verify Picks". The disconnect is visible without any interaction.
- **Triage hint:** One-line fix: change `{ href: "/attest", label: "Attest" }` at `web/components/Layout.tsx:18` to `{ href: "/attest", label: "Verify Picks" }`. This updates both the desktop nav and mobile hamburger menu simultaneously. LOW because the /attest page is operator-focused and not on the bettor golden path, but still visible to every first-time user on every page.

### 2026-04-22 16:10 UTC — MED — Footer jargon "Bittensor Subnet 103" + "Web Attestation" appears unsupported on every page

- [x] (fixed in 0d57fe17) Replaced footer "Bittensor Subnet 103." with "Powered by a decentralized network." and renamed nav link "Web Attestation" → "Verify Picks". Option 2 from triage hint (plain English, not tooltip) chosen for World Cup first-timer audience. Technical detail remains on /docs and /about.
- **Symptom:** Every page (home, /genius, /idiot, /leaderboard, /network, /docs, /about) displays in the footer: "Bittensor Subnet 103." and a nav link labeled "Web Attestation". Neither term has a tooltip or plain-English explanation in the footer context. A first-time sports bettor visiting djinn.gg sees "Bittensor Subnet 103" in the footer of the landing page and has no context for what it means. The `Tooltip.tsx` component has a definition for "Bittensor" but it is NOT wired to the footer text.
- **Diagnosis:** `web/components/Layout.tsx:253` renders `<p>...Bittensor Subnet 103.</p>` as raw text with no `<Tooltip>` wrapper. The `Tooltip.tsx:12` entry for "Bittensor" exists but is only usable when explicitly imported and wrapped. The "Web Attestation" nav link in the footer points to `/attest` — a technical term that a bettor would not recognize. On the network page, the table column header "Shamir" (`web/app/network/page.tsx:164`) also appears without an inline tooltip (only a `title=""` attribute, which doesn't render on mobile).
- **Repro:** Visit https://djinn.gg (anonymous, desktop or mobile). Scroll to footer. "Bittensor Subnet 103." is visible with no explanation. Click nothing — the jargon is there before any interaction.
- **Triage hint:** Two options: (1) wrap the footer "Bittensor Subnet 103" text in a `<Tooltip term="Bittensor">` component (2-line fix); (2) replace with plain English e.g. "Powered by a decentralized network." and move the technical detail to /docs. Option 2 is better for World Cup audience. "Web Attestation" nav label could become "Verify Picks" to match sports-bettor vocabulary. Network page "Shamir" column is a separate LOW finding below — lower priority since /network is for operators not bettors.
- **Screenshot:** e2e-screenshots/2026-04-22-heuristic-home.png (footer visible in full-page context), e2e-screenshots/2026-04-22-heuristic-genius.png.

### 2026-04-21 05:54 UTC — LOW — Base Sepolia RPC `eth_getLogs` 10k-block cap silently truncates djinn-e2e on-chain probes; chunked scan needed

- [x] (acknowledged, no product fix needed) Reported by `/djinn-e2e` iter-63 on-chain probe. Tooling-improvement for /djinn-e2e skill only — djinn-e2e background script already uses chunked scan per its skill instructions; the SC/Audit/OV zero-event baseline is unaffected. Closing as acknowledged.
- **Symptom:** `cast logs --from-block $((BLK-60000)) --to-block $BLK --address <ADDR>` against `https://sepolia.base.org` returns `Error: HTTP error 413 with body: {"jsonrpc":"2.0","error":{"code":-32614,"message":"eth_getLogs is limited to a 10,000 range"}}`. The wrapper script in `/djinn-e2e` background mode counts events with `grep -c '^- '` which returns `0` on the empty stdout (cast errored to stderr). Result: iter-58→62 reported "0 events across SC/Escrow/Col/Audit/OV in 60k window" — but the real Escrow/Collateral counts may have been non-zero (iter-63 chunked scan found 19 Escrow + 3 Collateral). The SC/Audit/OV zero claim is likely still accurate (those events would have shown up in the 10k chunks too), but the historical baseline for Escrow/Collateral was undercounted.
- **Diagnosis:** `cast logs` doesn't auto-chunk; the public Base Sepolia RPC enforces a 10k-block max range per `eth_getLogs` call. Other RPC providers (Alchemy, Infura) cap at 2k-10k; the public endpoint at `https://sepolia.base.org` is 10k. The `/djinn-e2e` wrapper needs to either (a) iterate in 10k chunks, or (b) use `cast logs --from-block latest -B 10000` natively, or (c) switch to an Alchemy/QuickNode RPC URL with higher limits, or (d) just always probe the most-recent 10k window and label it "~2.7h" instead of claiming 60k = "~16h".
- **Repro:**
  1. `export PATH=$PATH:/home/user/.foundry/bin && cast logs --rpc-url https://sepolia.base.org --from-block $((CURRENT-60000)) --to-block CURRENT --address 0xb43BA175a6784973eB3825acF801Cd7920ac692a 2>&1 | head -5`
  2. Output: `Error: HTTP error 413 with body: {"jsonrpc":"2.0","error":{"code":-32614,"message":"eth_getLogs is limited to a 10,000 range"},"id":0}`
  3. Wrapper line `N=$(... | grep -c '^- ')` → N=0, masking the failure.
- **Triage hint:** Cheapest fix — change the `/djinn-e2e` skill instructions to scan in 6×10k chunks (loop 0..5, FROM=BLK-(i+1)*10000, TO=BLK-i*10000) and sum. Or add `--max-priority-fee-per-gas` style chunking flag to the wrapper. **Skill change only — no product code.** Touches `/home/user/.claude/skills/djinn-e2e/SKILL.md` (or the equivalent skill instructions file). Outside the scope of `/djinn` to fix; should be flagged for /djinn-e2e skill author (= operator). Filing as LOW because the SC/Audit/OV signal that gates P0-01 closure is unaffected (zero in any window).

### 2026-04-21 05:10 UTC — MED — Vercel Attack Mode escalates to hard-403 'Error: Forbidden' during sequential Playwright runs; 90s cooldown+reload no longer resolves

- [x] (fixed in v1563) Reported by `/djinn-e2e` iter-62 regression. 3 tests failed at `helpers/navigate.ts:142`: `visitor.spec.ts:25:7` (test 74/74), `resilience.spec.ts:37:7` (test 68/74), `purchase.spec.ts:70:7` (mid-test, idiot browser context).
- **Symptom:** All 3 failures throw `Vercel hard-403 'Error: Forbidden' persisted after 90s cooldown.` The on-page DOM shows `<strong>Error:</strong> Forbidden` and `<strong>403</strong>: Forbidden` with a Vercel request ID (`1776740747-ycEspaMRu1BwbKomCENPEEngw4sXNpBX` for purchase, `1776740840-2hvCRZ3EwhkUYf1F9bfFa8YLr7OdOgrg` for resilience; visitor had a similar ID). Redirect URL observed: `https://www.djinn.gg/` (auto-redirect from BASE_URL `https://djinn.gg`). iter-61 documented "2 checkpoint events auto-resolved mid-run"; iter-62 has ZERO auto-resolved and 3 escalated to hard-403.
- **Diagnosis:** Three compounding factors, ranked:
  1. **Playwright fingerprint escalation at tail of sequential run** — tests 68/74 and 74/74 are near the end of a single 8.8-min sequential worker. Vercel Attack Mode fingerprinting may accumulate evidence across navigation events within one session, flipping from "soft JS challenge" (resolvable via saved cookies) to "hard fingerprint block" (`/_vercel/security/request-challenge` 403) past a threshold. Iter-49/51 empirically placed the recovery window at 60-90s; this iter suggests the WAF rules tightened OR the fingerprint accumulation now produces a longer block window than 90s.
  2. **Cross-context cookie isolation** — `purchase.spec.ts:70` uses `browser.newContext()` for BOTH the genius and idiot sides. The storage-state file (`e2e/ux/.vercel-session.json`) is loaded into the FIRST context (genius) via `playwright.ux.config.ts`, but each subsequent `browser.newContext()` call starts with FRESH cookies. When the idiot context calls `landOnSite`, it hits Vercel with no session — exact scenario where hard-403 triggers if WAF rate-limits the source IP.
  3. **`www.djinn.gg` redirect** — the hard-403 URL in both failures is `https://www.djinn.gg/`, not `https://djinn.gg/`. If Vercel Attack Mode is keyed per-apex-domain vs per-canonical-host, the redirect hop may be evaluated as a fresh request without the cookies set on the `djinn.gg` origin. Worth checking whether `BASE_URL` should point directly at `https://www.djinn.gg` to avoid the redirect and its cookie-drop side effect.
- **Repro:**
  1. Clean shell, 8-10 min of Playwright ux-journeys run against `https://djinn.gg`
  2. Tests 68+ hit `landOnSite` fresh (or in a new context)
  3. Vercel returns `403 Forbidden` HTML page with `Error: Forbidden` and a request ID
  4. Playwright's `isVercelHardBlock` detects this, waits 90s, reloads, re-checks — still 403
  5. Test throws at `navigate.ts:142`
- **Triage hint:** Three paths /djinn could take, in order of least-to-most intrusive:
  1. **Extend cooldown + add exponential backoff** in `navigate.ts:139-150` — 90s → 180s + retry-3 with `page.context().clearCookies()` between retries might get past the single-session fingerprint accumulation. Cheapest fix.
  2. **Set `VERCEL_BYPASS_SECRET`** as a persistent env var on the dev box. `playwright.ux.config.ts` already reads it in `landOnSite` Layer 1 — if set, all tests bypass the checkpoint entirely. This is the cleanest fix; Vercel Settings > Security > Bypass Secret.
  3. **Point `BASE_URL` at `https://www.djinn.gg`** to skip the apex→www redirect hop that may be dropping cookies. Low-risk experiment.
  4. **Run each spec in isolated worker** — `workers: 1` is already set, but each test-file starts in its own worker if `fullyParallel=false` and `projects` are split. Harder lift.
- **Screenshot:** `e2e-screenshots/iter-62-visitor-hard403-2026-04-21.png` (visitor failure, same 403 UI as the other 2); `e2e-screenshots/iter-62-resilience-hard403-2026-04-21.png`

### 2026-04-21 05:10 UTC — MED — `/health` does not expose `DJINN_FF_BATCH_SETTLEMENT_HTTP_SUBMIT` feature flag state, blocking P0-01 closure observability

- [x] (fixed in v1562) Reported by `/djinn-e2e` iter-62 source reading + direct SSH probe of UID 0 `.env`. Exact analog of iter-61 `shamir_threshold` visibility LOW finding, but MED severity because this IS the current P0-01 critical-path gate.
- **Symptom:** v1558 (commit `e8996983`) added feature flag `DJINN_FF_BATCH_SETTLEMENT_HTTP_SUBMIT` (default OFF per `validator/djinn_validator/feature_flags.py:167`) to promote `try_shadow_distributed_settlement` from observe-only to actually calling `submitVote` on-chain. Even after 4-of-6 validators pull v1558+ code (UID 0/1/86/189 at v1558+X per iter-62 probe), UID 0 is the ONLY validator **verified** to have the FF set (SSH into UID 0: `DJINN_FF_BATCH_SETTLEMENT_HTTP=true` + `DJINN_FF_BATCH_SETTLEMENT_HTTP_SUBMIT=true` in `/root/djinn/validator/.env`). Non-operator validators (UID 1, 86, 189) may have the code but not the `.env` update — zero way to verify from `/health`. The `HealthResponse` dataclass in `validator/djinn_validator/api/server.py:2529-2553` exposes `version, shamir_threshold, audit_min_batch_size, settlement_contract, settlement_registered` but NOT FF state. Result: `OutcomeVoting` quorum-in-submit-enabled-mode is effectively 1 (UID 0 only), well below `quorumThreshold=4`, so zero on-chain settlement despite 4 validators on the right code.
- **Diagnosis:** `validator/djinn_validator/api/server.py:521` already imports `feature_flags.flags as _startup_flags` and logs `"feature_flags_active"` at startup — so the FF state is computed fleet-wide, just not surfaced on `/health`. Minimal fix: add `feature_flags: dict[str, bool]` (or specific fields `batch_settlement_http: bool` + `batch_settlement_http_submit: bool`) to `HealthResponse` + populate from `flags` in the `health()` handler. Web plumbing: `web/lib/networkStatus.ts` → `web/app/network/page.tsx` + `web/app/network/validator/[uid]/view.tsx` to add a column/row showing the submit-enabled state per validator. Pattern is exactly `settlement_contract` / `shamir_threshold` surface-gap fixes from iter-5 and iter-61.
- **Repro:**
  1. Identify validators on v1558+ via `/health.version` (UID 0/1/86/189 at time of iter-62 probe)
  2. Attempt to observe `DJINN_FF_BATCH_SETTLEMENT_HTTP_SUBMIT` state per validator — no API surface exists
  3. Only recourse: SSH into each box and `grep DJINN_FF_BATCH_SETTLEMENT_HTTP /root/djinn/validator/.env`
  4. Without FF=true on ≥4 validators, shadow_settle returns `ShadowSettleSubmitData` but the submit branch in `main.py:528` is skipped, so `submitVote` is never called on-chain; OutcomeVoting sees 0 votes; no batch finalizes
- **Triage hint:** Follow the pattern `/djinn` used for `fb270f50 v1454: expose audit_min_batch_size + shamir_threshold on /health`. Two-commit structure: (1) validator-side expose FF state in `/health` (one file, one dataclass field, one handler line); (2) web-side surface it in `/network` table + per-UID detail page. The OV badge pattern from `web/components/network/OvBadge.tsx` works for a `SubmitEnabledBadge.tsx` — green if `batch_settlement_http_submit && batch_settlement_http`, orange if either missing, tooltip linking to the v1558 deploy note. **Why MED not LOW:** P0-01 closure depends on 4+ validators having this FF set. Until operators can verify fleet-wide FF state from the product UI, closing P0-01 requires out-of-band ops coordination — which is the slowest step in every P0-01 sub-blocker closure so far (operator pulls of v1455 took ~4 iters, v1556 took ~2 iters, same pattern incoming for v1558 FF rollout unless surfaced). **Why not separate CRITICAL:** the protocol works correctly given correct FF state; this is observability, not a correctness bug.
- **Cross-reference:** iter-61 LOW finding (shamir_threshold visibility — same pattern, LOW because the shamir_threshold gate had fewer sub-steps; this FF gate is the current critical path).

### 2026-04-21 04:53 UTC — LOW — `/network` UI does not expose per-validator `shamir_threshold`, leaving v1556-stranded validators invisible to operators

- [x] (fixed in v1562) Reported by `/djinn-e2e` iter-61 heuristic sweep + source grep. Pattern is analogous to the iter-5 `settlement_contract` drift surface gap (closed by `8be1881` + `d4a1126`).
- **Symptom:** `/network` page and `/network/validator/[uid]` detail page show `version`, `settlement_contract`, `settlement_diagnosis`, `peers`, `outcomes_resolved_total`, `pending_outcomes`, etc. — but NOT `shamir_threshold`. With v1556 (commit `4f50d532`) flipping the validator default `SHAMIR_THRESHOLD` 7→2 to match client `SHAMIR_MIN=SHAMIR_MAX=2`, validators that haven't pulled v1556 are stranded at `st=7` and silently silent-skip Shamir reconstruction. Operators of UID 86 (v1455+1) and UID 213 (v999) currently have **zero in-product signal** that they're misconfigured — they have to read MAINNET_BLOCKERS.md or run a direct `curl http://<their-ip>:8421/health | jq .shamir_threshold` to discover it.
- **Diagnosis:** Validator `/health` already exposes `shamir_threshold` (verified via direct probe — full key list above in HEADLINE D). The data plumbing path is `web/lib/networkStatus.ts` (`ValidatorHealthSnapshot` interface + per-validator `/health` fetch + projection) → `web/app/api/network/status/route.ts` (API surface) → `web/app/network/page.tsx` (table) + `web/app/network/validator/[uid]/view.tsx` (detail). Adding `shamir_threshold` is mechanical: extend the projection in `networkStatus.ts`, add a column to the network table, render a row on the detail page. Optional polish: a small "stranded ⚠️" badge analogous to the OV badge for any validator where `shamir_threshold > client SHAMIR_MAX` (pulling client SHAMIR_MAX from the same env path the commit route uses).
- **Repro:**
  1. `curl -s --max-time 8 http://192.150.253.122:8421/health | jq '.shamir_threshold'` → `7` (UID 86 — stranded)
  2. `curl -s --max-time 8 http://3.150.72.96:8421/health | jq '.shamir_threshold'` → `7` (UID 213 — stranded)
  3. `curl -s --max-time 8 http://37.60.251.252:8421/health | jq '.shamir_threshold'` → `2` (UID 0 — healthy)
  4. Visit `https://djinn.gg/network` → no `shamir_threshold` column visible. Click into UID 86 detail → no `shamir_threshold` row visible.
- **Triage hint:** Follow the same pattern `/djinn` used for `8be1881` (web: add OV settlement column to /network) + `d4a1126` (web: show settlement diagnosis on validator detail page). The `OvBadge.tsx` component (`web/components/network/OvBadge.tsx`) is a good pattern reference — a small `ShamirBadge.tsx` could render `st=2 ✓` (green) or `st=7 ⚠️ stranded` (orange) with a tooltip linking to the v1556 deploy notes. **Why not HIGH:** the on-chain mismatch is already detectable by `/djinn` via direct probe and tracked in MAINNET_BLOCKERS P0-01; this is operator-facing visibility, not a protocol bug. Saves one cycle of operator-outreach friction once shipped.

### 2026-04-20 16:54 UTC — HIGH — Every non-validator subdomain on djinn.gg serves the wrong TLS certificate

- [x] Fixed 2026-04-20 21:32Z by adding a catch-all regex server block `~^(?:.+\.)?djinn\.gg$` to `scripts/nginx-wildcard-djinn-gg.conf` (and the live `/etc/nginx/sites-enabled/wildcard-djinn-gg.conf` on VPS #1 37.60.251.252). The new block serves `/etc/letsencrypt/wildcard-djinn-gg/fullchain.pem` (SANs `*.djinn.gg, djinn.gg`) and returns 404; HTTP side 301s to `https://www.djinn.gg$request_uri`. First-matching-regex rule preserves `v<uid>.djinn.gg` → validator routing because the validator regex appears earlier in the file. Verified post-reload: `api/attest/app/admin/auth/dashboard/docs/randomxyz.djinn.gg` all now return `subject=CN = *.djinn.gg` + HTTPS 404; `v0.djinn.gg/health` still proxies to UID 0. The shared-VPS concern (diagnosis #3) — cousin-project `00-default.conf` still serves proveaudit.com cert for arbitrary non-djinn Hosts pointed at our IP — is intentionally unchanged; it's the correct behavior for foreign Hosts. Phishing amplifier surface is closed: a typo'd djinn.gg share no longer shows a competitor brand.
- **Symptom:** Any `*.djinn.gg` subdomain that is not a validator (`v{N}.djinn.gg`) terminates TLS with a Let's Encrypt certificate whose `CN = proveaudit.com` and `subjectAltName = api.firmrecord.com, debust.com, firmrecord.com, proveaudit.com, www.debust.com, www.firmrecord.com, www.proveaudit.com`. Validated against 12 hosts including `api.djinn.gg`, `attest.djinn.gg`, `app.djinn.gg`, `admin.djinn.gg`, `auth.djinn.gg`, `dashboard.djinn.gg`, `docs.djinn.gg`, `blog.djinn.gg`, `proof.djinn.gg`, `prove.djinn.gg`, `proveaudit.djinn.gg`, and even a nonsense `randomxyz.djinn.gg` — all return the same `proveaudit.com` cert. Curl output: `curl: (60) SSL: no alternative certificate subject name matches target host name 'attest.djinn.gg'`. Real browsers render `NET::ERR_CERT_COMMON_NAME_INVALID` (Chrome) / `SEC_ERROR_UNKNOWN_ISSUER`-adjacent scary warning. When a user clicks through with `-k`, nginx then closes the HTTP/2 stream with `PROTOCOL_ERROR` — empty reply on HTTP/1.1 — so there is no content to serve either.
- **Diagnosis (ranked):** (1) **Most likely — nginx `default_server` fallback on 37.60.251.252 is the proveaudit.com vhost.** `dig +short attest.djinn.gg` → `37.60.251.252` (our VPS #1, shared with proveaudit/debust/firmrecord cousin projects). The wildcard cert for `*.djinn.gg` DOES exist on that box (v0/v1/v2/… serve `CN = *.djinn.gg` with SANs `*.djinn.gg, djinn.gg` — valid for ALL djinn.gg subdomains), and the matching nginx vhosts have explicit `server_name v{uid}.djinn.gg` directives. But no vhost claims the catchall, so unmatched Host headers fall through to whichever vhost has `default_server` set, which is the `proveaudit.com` one. Fix is one line: add `ssl_default_server` / `default_server` to the `*.djinn.gg` vhost (or add a `server { listen 443 ssl default_server; server_name _; return 404; }` block with the djinn wildcard cert). (2) **Less likely — DNS wildcard record `*.djinn.gg A 37.60.251.252` was set for the validator router but sweeps too broadly.** If the intent is only `v*.djinn.gg`, tightening the DNS record to `v*.djinn.gg A 37.60.251.252` plus removing the wildcard would eliminate the attack surface but also break `api.djinn.gg`/`attest.djinn.gg` if those are ever wanted for real. (3) **Edge case — the whole shared-VPS pattern is the bug.** Cousin projects (proveaudit/debust/firmrecord) on the same nginx instance mean any mis-typed djinn subdomain shows a different brand's cert to the user — confusing even when harmless, and a phishing vector if a malicious actor notices and sets up lookalikes pointed to the same IP. `feedback_decentralization_means_decentralized.md` is tangentially relevant (the SPOF is our VPS).
- **Repro:**
  ```bash
  for sub in api attest app dashboard auth admin docs blog randomxyz; do
    echo -n "$sub.djinn.gg: "
    echo | openssl s_client -servername $sub.djinn.gg -connect $sub.djinn.gg:443 2>/dev/null \
      | openssl x509 -noout -subject 2>&1 | head -1
  done
  # All 9 return: subject=CN = proveaudit.com
  curl -sSv https://attest.djinn.gg/ -m 10  # curl: (60) SSL: no alternative certificate subject name matches
  ```
- **Triage hint:** (a) No frontend code currently links to these subdomains (`grep -r 'api\.djinn\.gg\|attest\.djinn\.gg' web/` returns zero), so the direct-click blast radius is zero. (b) External blast radius is real: old tweets, blog posts, OG preview scrapers, or typo'd shares that land on `attest.djinn.gg` (a plausible subdomain for "Web Attestation") will show a Let's Encrypt cert for *a competitor's brand name*. (c) Phishing amplifier: today `api.djinn.gg` is a dead endpoint with a valid-but-wrong cert; tomorrow an attacker can publish `api-djinn.gg` or `djinn-api.net` with a matching cert and point people there, citing "api.djinn.gg is broken — use ours instead." Fixing the default_server closes this. (d) Check `/etc/nginx/sites-enabled/` on VPS #1 for which vhost has `default_server` — likely one of the `proveaudit.com` configs owns the `listen 443 ssl default_server`. (e) Related to (but distinct from) the IPFS/ENS decentralized fallback finding at iter-51: both show the hybrid architecture has gaps in how "non-canonical" hosts fail open.
- **Screenshot:** n/a (cert-level; visible via any browser navigating `https://attest.djinn.gg/`).

### 2026-04-20 16:54 UTC — LOW — Leaderboard ROI displays `+0.0%` for Geniuses with zero audits

- [x] Fixed 2026-04-20 21:43Z by v1450 (`web/app/leaderboard/page.tsx`). Gated both `QualityScore` render and ROI render on `entry.auditCount > 0`, falling back to `-` (same slate-400 dash the Win Rate column already used). Now all three columns (QS, ROI, Win Rate) consistently display `-` when a Genius has zero resolved audits, and real values once the first audit settles. Tooltip `title="No resolved audits yet"` on the dashes so a hovering user can see why. Mainly Cup-eve polish but the data-representation consistency matters: a Cup first-timer seeing "+0.0% track record" on a never-audited Genius was the worst case.
- [x] Reported by `/djinn-e2e` heuristic.
- **Symptom:** `https://www.djinn.gg/leaderboard` shows a two-row table (`0x68fc…2a1d`, `0x4986…11d3`). Both rows display ROI = `+0.0%` (green, with explicit plus sign) despite `Audits = 0` and `Proofs = 0` for both. The adjacent `Win Rate` column correctly uses `—` to represent "no resolved audits yet", but ROI does not — it presents as a positive finite return, which a sports bettor reading the leaderboard interprets as "this Genius is flat/break-even" rather than "this Genius has no verified record yet."
- **Diagnosis (ranked):** (1) **Most likely — view treats `roi === 0` as a displayable zero rather than as sentinel for "no data"**. Quality Score shows `QS 0` which may be a matching miscoding — the actual QS formula (`Favorable: +Notional × (odds − 1)`, `Unfavorable: −Notional × Backing%`) only produces a real 0 when favorable and unfavorable sum to 0, which is structurally impossible with zero audits (numerator is 0 / 0 = undefined). Data shows the backend is emitting `0` instead of `null`. (2) **Less likely — ROI is correctly 0 because the formula divides by `notional_staked` which is nonzero across unresolved audits.** But with `Audits = 0` there's no resolved pool to compute against, so this still reduces to diagnosis (1). (3) **Edge case — the display intentionally distinguishes "+0.0% tracked" vs "—"**. If that's the intent, it is unexplained to the user; no tooltip or footnote clarifies.
- **Repro:** Navigate `https://www.djinn.gg/leaderboard` (www subdomain, apex hits Vercel challenge). Observe table: Genius 1 shows `QS 0`, `Signals 1230`, `Audits 0`, `ROI +0.0%`, `Proofs 0`, `Win Rate —`. Inconsistency is between ROI (`+0.0%`) and Win Rate (`—`) given identical `Audits = 0` inputs.
- **Triage hint:** The fix is probably one ternary in the leaderboard row component: `roi === null || auditCount === 0 ? '—' : formatPct(roi)`. Either treat `ROI` like `Win Rate` (render `—` when `Audits = 0`) or add a tooltip explaining "ROI computed across 0 resolved audits → placeholder zero". The second-order question is whether the **Quality Score column** has the same bug — it also shows `QS 0` with zero audits, same root cause as ROI. Worth checking if they share a formatter. Mainly polish, but with World Cup 54 days out and leaderboard being the primary trust signal for buyers, correctly showing "no track record yet" vs "flat track record" matters for first-time users deciding who to purchase from.
- **Screenshot:** `e2e-screenshots/heuristic-2026-04-20-leaderboard.png`

### 2026-04-20 08:13 UTC — HIGH — v1402 "Bet size → Notional" revert MISSED `/leaderboard` page: 2 forbidden-phrase strings still leak at `web/app/leaderboard/page.tsx:244,247` under "How Quality Score Works" heading; trips parameterized.spec.ts forbidden-phrase sweep on both desktop + mobile (2/2 iter-44 failures on same root cause)

- [x] (duplicate of CRITICAL finding from iter-44 at line 881; both closed by v1406 · commit `8e89bf99` · 2-line edit to `web/app/leaderboard/page.tsx:244,247` "Bet size" → "Notional"; grep confirms zero remaining site-wide occurrences) Reported by `/djinn-e2e` regression (`parameterized.spec.ts:133:13` iter-1 desktop leaderboard AND iter-1 mobile leaderboard). Both assertions fail on `/\bbet\s*size\b/i` matching DOM text — same regex v1402 itself added in the FORBIDDEN_PHRASES array (`web/e2e/ux/journeys/parameterized.spec.ts:43-44`). **v1402 is both the shipper of the regex AND the author of the miss** — the regex caught what the revert missed.
- **Symptom:** Regression output shows:
  ```
  Error: [desktop leaderboard] "\bbet\s*size\b" leaked into DOM at https://www.djinn.gg/leaderboard
  Expected pattern: not /\bbet\s*size\b/i
  Received string: "… How Quality Score Works … Favorable: +Bet size × (odds − 1) / Unfavorable: −Bet size × Backing% …"
  ```
  Identical failure on mobile viewport. Two strings, both under the pedagogical "How Quality Score Works" section:
  - `page.tsx:244`: `<span className="text-green-600">Favorable:</span> +Bet size &times; (odds &minus; 1)`
  - `page.tsx:247`: `<span className="text-red-600">Unfavorable:</span> &minus;Bet size &times; Backing%`
  `grep -rniE '\bbet.?size\b' web/app web/components web/lib` returns ONLY these 2 lines site-wide — confirmed v1402 reverted all others.
- **Diagnosis:** v1402 (commit `7ce353d4`, "revert notional → 'bet size' rename; protect legal positioning") swept 17 user-surface strings across `/idiot/signal`, `/idiot/page`, `/idiot/browse`, `/genius/signal/new`, `/genius/signal/page` — but the `/leaderboard` page's "How Quality Score Works" pedagogical section was NOT in that sweep. The scoring formula explanation is a standalone copy-block on the leaderboard page that v1402's file list didn't include. v1402's commit message explicitly says "Djinn is an information marketplace, not a sportsbook" and that the whitepaper "defines notional as a reference amount that 'does not need to match any actual wager'". Calling the score-formula input a "Bet size" directly contradicts this legal positioning on one of the site's highest-traffic top-nav pages (/leaderboard is 3rd in the main nav after Home, Genius).
- **Repro:**
  1. Fast: `grep -rniE '\bbet.?size\b' /home/user/djinn/web/app /home/user/djinn/web/components /home/user/djinn/web/lib` — returns exactly 2 lines, both in `web/app/leaderboard/page.tsx`.
  2. Browser: `curl -s https://www.djinn.gg/leaderboard | grep -oiE 'bet[ -]?size'` — returns "Bet size" strings.

### 2026-04-20 04:08 UTC — HIGH — Late-suite hard-403 cluster from Vercel: 3 regression journeys fail with "403: Forbidden" page (NOT the JS challenge); `landOnSite()`'s title check misses this distinct page, surfaces as confusing title-mismatch errors

- [x] (fixed in v1397) Reported by `/djinn-e2e` regression (visitor.spec.ts:44, purchase.spec.ts:179, resilience.spec.ts:60). `landOnSite()` now detects Vercel's hard-403 page title via `isVercelHardBlock()`, cools 30s + reloads once, and throws an actionable "Playwright-class fingerprint block" error if still hard-blocked — so iteration reports no longer present the bug as confusing `toHaveTitle` mismatches. Cooldown chosen at 30s (not the 60s suggested) because the 36s-cluster window observed in iter-41 means 30s should let the softening path resume; if 30s proves too short on a future iter, bump it.
- **Symptom:** Three back-to-back Playwright tests at the END of a 6-minute regression run fail with the page title `"403: Forbidden"` instead of `/djinn/i`. The page body is a plain Vercel error page: `Error: Forbidden` / `403: Forbidden` / `ID: <request-id>` — NO `<script>` blocks, NO challenge JS, NO retry mechanism. Vercel request IDs `1776657966-cfvUUYdfiE5VOEfvrW74MMXhWPn6ouUz` (purchase), `1776657978-gFZW8YaTyB0HE6aSbBTqIL62FHXlk8Dc` (resilience), `1776658002-TVrqSr7QQMuqwlKk0bM9I1tQ3SZnSRtf` (visitor) cluster within 36 seconds. The 58 earlier tests in the same run passed cleanly through normal Vercel JS challenge auto-resolution.
- **Diagnosis:** Two-layer issue.
  - **Layer 1 (Vercel-side):** Sustained Playwright traffic from the test runner's IP eventually trips a Vercel WAF rule that escalates from "show JS challenge" to "hard-403 with no challenge". This is Vercel's anti-bot defense kicking up a level. Confirmed: immediately after the test run, `curl -A 'Mozilla/5.0 ... Safari/605.1.15' https://djinn.gg/` STILL returns the normal `Vercel Security Checkpoint` JS challenge with `x-vercel-mitigated: challenge` header — meaning curl-class clients are NOT hard-blocked, only the specific Playwright-class fingerprint that ran 58+ requests in 6 minutes. This is **NOT user-impacting today** because real human visitors don't trigger the Playwright fingerprint detection.
  - **Layer 2 (harness-side, the actual fix target):** `web/e2e/ux/helpers/navigate.ts:65-83` `waitForCheckpoint()` returns false unless `document.title.includes("Vercel Security Checkpoint")`. The hard-403 page title is `"403: Forbidden"`, so `landOnSite()` thinks the page loaded successfully, hands control back to the test, and the test then fails on `expect(page).toHaveTitle(/djinn/i)` with a confusing "got '403: Forbidden'" error. The harness should detect this distinct page and fail-fast with a clear "Vercel hard-blocked the test runner IP — wait 5-10min and retry" message, OR auto-pause and retry once after a longer cooldown (curl evidence above shows the JS challenge mode resumes within seconds, so a 30-60s cooldown + retry would likely succeed).
- **Repro:**
  1. Run the full UX regression: `cd /home/user/djinn/web && set -a && . ./.env.local && set +a && xvfb-run -a npx playwright test --config playwright.ux.config.ts`.
  2. Watch tests 60-74 of 74. If the run is the second consecutive sweep within ~30 min, observe ≥1 of {visitor, purchase, resilience, mobile-resilience} fail with title `"403: Forbidden"`.
  3. Counter-check from same shell: `curl -A 'Mozilla/5.0 (Macintosh; Intel Mac OS X 14_0) AppleWebKit/605.1.15 (KHTML, like Gecko) Version/17.1 Safari/605.1.15' -sSI https://djinn.gg/ | grep -i mitigated` → returns `x-vercel-mitigated: challenge` (curl path still gets JS challenge, NOT hard 403).
  4. Wait 5-10 minutes and re-run only the failed tests: `xvfb-run -a npx playwright test --config playwright.ux.config.ts -g "browse the site like a first-time visitor"` → typically passes.
- **Triage hint:**
  - **Minimal fix (harness, ~10 LOC)**: in `web/e2e/ux/helpers/navigate.ts` `landOnSite()`, after `waitForCheckpoint()` returns and before the existing checks, add:
    ```ts
    const titleAfter = await page.title();
    if (/^403/i.test(titleAfter) || titleAfter.toLowerCase().includes("forbidden")) {
      console.log("[ux] Vercel hard-blocked the runner IP. Cooling down 60s and retrying once...");
      await page.waitForTimeout(60_000);
      await page.goto(baseUrl, { waitUntil: "domcontentloaded" });
      const stillBlocked = /^403/i.test(await page.title()) || (await page.title()).toLowerCase().includes("forbidden");
      if (stillBlocked) {
        throw new Error("Vercel hard-403 — runner IP rate-limited. Wait 5-10min before retry, OR set VERCEL_BYPASS_SECRET to bypass.");
      }
    }
    ```
    This converts the cluster of confusing test failures into one clear runner-side error message + a single auto-retry. Most cases will pass on retry per curl evidence above.
  - **Don't try to "fix" Vercel-side** — the WAF behavior is correct and protective. The fix is making our harness fail gracefully and recover where possible.
  - **Severity rationale**: HIGH because it broke an 8-iter streak and confuses operators (the title-mismatch error doesn't tell you to wait/retry; it looks like a real product bug). Could be downgraded to MED after the harness fix lands since real users are unaffected. Would NOT be CRITICAL — the user-facing site works fine.
  - **Cross-reference**: this is a different failure mode than iter-13/15/16/17/18's MCP-playwright Code-29 checkpoint (which is MCP-Chromium-specific and totally blocks even ad-hoc browsing). The hard-403 here is `xvfb-run` real-Chromium-specific and only triggers under sustained automation traffic.
  - **`VERCEL_BYPASS_SECRET` would solve this entirely** — if set, `landOnSite()` line 107 sets `x-vercel-protection-bypass` header which skips the WAF. This is the iter-1+ standing recommendation; the operator hasn't set it because it's a secret-in-CI question. Worth re-evaluating now that we have a track record showing automated runs do periodically trip the hard-403 path.
- **Screenshot:** `e2e-screenshots/2026-04-20-visitor-403-forbidden.png` (visitor test landing page), `e2e-screenshots/2026-04-20-purchase-idiot-nav-missing.png` (purchase test, same hard-403), `e2e-screenshots/2026-04-20-resilience-genius-nav-missing.png` (resilience test, same hard-403).

### 2026-04-20 02:10 UTC — MED — `is_registered_validator()` returns False on RPC exception → misleading `missing_bootstrap` diagnosis when chain flaps

- [x] Fixed in v1383. `is_registered_validator()` + `is_registered_validator_at()` now return `bool | None`, returning `None` on RPC exception; `main.py` startup probe and `server.py` /health diagnosis both add explicit `None` handling (`rpc_flap_inconclusive`) so a flappy RPC no longer pages an operator with a spurious `missing_bootstrap` remediation.
- **Symptom:** v189 intermittently reports `"settlement_registered": false, "settlement_diagnosis": "missing_bootstrap", "settlement_remediation": "Signer is not registered on the canonical OutcomeVoting. Operator must run AddValidatorSigner.s.sol (with SIGNER=0xDeF83fbCe82b469a95a5704019ed2E522A924A98)..."`. Remediation text instructs the operator to bootstrap a signer that is **already bootstrapped on-chain**.
- **Diagnosis:** `validator/djinn_validator/chain/contracts.py:1594-1606` — `is_registered_validator()` wraps the `isValidator()` web3 call in a try/except that swallows ALL exceptions and returns `False`. When the RPC flaps (timeout, 5xx, `-32603`, reverted-with-data, etc.), the method can't distinguish "RPC transiently failed" from "signer genuinely not in set". Downstream logic at `validator/djinn_validator/api/server.py:2274-2301` sees `settlement_registered is False` and pairs it with `is_registered_validator_at(canonical)` — which has the same bug at `contracts.py:1636-1638` (catches exception, returns False) — so both probes can hit the same RPC blip and both get False. Result: diagnostic flips to `missing_bootstrap` with a remediation that tells the operator to re-run a script they already ran. Verified on v189 today: `cast call 0xAD534f…643e5 "isValidator(address)(bool)" 0xDeF83fbCe82b469a95a5704019ed2E522A924A98 --rpc-url https://sepolia.base.org` returns `true`, but v189's `/health` returns `settlement_registered=false` on ~25-30% of probes. The signer IS registered; the RPC is flaky.
- **Repro:**
  ```
  for i in $(seq 1 8); do
    curl -sS -m 5 "https://v189.djinn.gg/health" | jq -c '{cc:.chain_connected, sr:.settlement_registered, diag:.settlement_diagnosis}'
    sleep 3
  done
  # Expect 5-6/8 green (cc:true, sr:true, diag:null) and 2-3/8 degraded (cc:false|missing, sr:false, diag:"missing_bootstrap")
  ```
  Counter-check: `cast call 0xAD534f4CAB13707BD4d65e4EF086A455e6A643e5 "isValidator(address)(bool)" 0xDeF83fbCe82b469a95a5704019ed2E522A924A98 --rpc-url https://sepolia.base.org` always returns `true`.
- **Triage hint:**
  - **Minimal fix**: change the return type of `is_registered_validator()` (and its sibling `is_registered_validator_at()`) from `bool` to `bool | None` and return `None` on exception instead of `False`. Propagate that up through `server.py:2267` — if `settlement_registered is None`, skip the diagnosis branch entirely (line 2274 already guards on `is False`, so changing it to only diagnose on explicit False is a 1-line change).
  - **Caller update**: `server.py:2274` already uses `is False` (not `not`), so that check naturally ignores `None`. The flow at 4519 also returns None on exception — keep that pattern.
  - **Don't mask the flap**: the fact that v189's chain is flapping is a real ops signal. Keeping `chain_connected: false` unchanged is correct. The bug is specifically in conflating "RPC error during signer check" with "signer confirmed absent".
  - **Not iter-37 redux**: iter-37's HIGH finding was about v189 having a stale `.env`  `OUTCOME_VOTING_ADDRESS`. That bug is FIXED (v189 now reports canonical `0xAD534f…643e5` consistently). The current flap is a **different** bug: validator code pattern, not operator config. Marking iter-37 closed was correct; this is a new defect exposed by the iter-39 operator restart putting v189 back on the canonical path where the flakiness now surfaces.
  - **Bonus**: worth grep-auditing all `except Exception` → `return False` patterns in `contracts.py` to see if other read-path methods have the same conflation. e.g., `is_cycle_finalized_failed` at line 1590 does the same thing — less user-impacting, but ditto semantic drift from "don't know" to "definitely false".
  - **Severity rationale**: MED because (a) zero user-facing impact today — v1380's parallel race routes `/v1/idiot/browse` away from v189, and `/network` page only uses `settlement_contract` (not `settlement_registered`) for its validator table; (b) operator-facing impact is real — a misled operator might run `AddValidatorSigner.s.sol` unnecessarily (harmless but noisy), or worse, interpret the diagnosis as "our validator is misbehaving" and page someone. - [x] (closed 2026-04-21 per DEV-045 hybrid decision — /api/delegates stays Vercel-routed; validator-direct /v1/network/delegates returning `{}` is cosmetic gap only, no operator-push needed). Reported by `/djinn-e2e` heuristic (`curl https://v<uid>.djinn.gg/v1/network/delegates` sweep across v0/v1/v2/v86/v189/v213).
- **Symptom:** All 4 validators that have the endpoint registered (v0, v2, v189, v213 — the v1378+ serving set, plus v213 via its stale-v999-but-fresh-binary) return HTTP 200 with a 2-byte body `{}`. v1 + v86 return 404 `{"detail":"Not Found"}` (endpoint not yet wired up on their pre-v1378 binaries — expected). Today this is cosmetic because the frontend's `/network` page still calls `fetch("/api/delegates")` (Vercel-proxied to taostats, see `web/app/network/page.tsx:307`). But if/when DEV-045's hybrid approach cuts `/api/delegates` over to validator-direct (same pattern as `BROWSER_BOOTSTRAP_HOSTS` in `web/lib/api.ts:323`), delegate names across the `/network` validator + miner tables disappear network-wide because no validator has `TAOSTATS_API_KEY` in its env.
- **Diagnosis:** The `/v1/network/delegates` handler at `validator/djinn_validator/api/server.py:3718-3744` explicitly returns `{}` + 200 when `TAOSTATS_API_KEY` env var is missing (docstring at line 3729 codifies this as "a UX nice-to-have, not a correctness requirement"). Zero validators have the env var set. Fleet-wide operator gap — not a code bug, an infra/onboarding gap.
- **Repro:**
  ```
  for uid in 0 1 2 86 189 213; do
    echo -n "v$uid: "
    curl -sS -m 8 -w "HTTP %{http_code} %{size_download}B\n" -o /dev/null "https://v$uid.djinn.gg/v1/network/delegates"
  done
  ```
  Returns `HTTP 200 2B` on v0/v2/v189/v213 (empty `{}`) and `HTTP 404 22B` on v1/v86.
- **Triage hint:**
  - **Before cutting `/api/delegates` over to validator-direct**: either (a) publish an operator-facing doc (e.g., append to `docs/operator-guide.md` if one exists, or add a README note under `validator/`) instructing every SN103 operator to set `TAOSTATS_API_KEY=<key>` in their validator `.env`, OR (b) keep `/api/delegates` routed through Vercel in the hybrid model since names are cosmetic and the Vercel route already works — this is cheapest and aligns with the "Vercel = serve layer" framing from `project_hybrid_vercel_ipfs_decision.md`.
  - **If (a) is chosen**: also consider whether having `TAOSTATS_API_KEY` on every validator is a privacy/quota concern (every validator would hit taostats independently, multiplying quota consumption by N validators for what's effectively cached data).
  - **Not an operator-push for iter-39**: user impact is zero today (names render via Vercel route). Filing as LOW to capture the latent risk; `/djinn` can decide between (a) fleet onboarding or (b) keeping Vercel in the delegate-name path.
  - `/v1/network/delegates` is one of only a handful of validator endpoints that depend on external API credentials (most others use only chain + bittensor state). Worth auditing whether other endpoints have similar latent deps.
- **Severity rationale:** LOW because (a) zero user impact today — Vercel route still covers it, (b) the hybrid decision (per `project_hybrid_vercel_ipfs_decision.md`) explicitly keeps Vercel as the serve layer, so this may never surface if delegates stay Vercel-side, (c) discovery is cheap (a 3-line shell loop) and the fix is either a doc update OR a routing decision, neither urgent. Would escalate to MED if the frontend starts calling `/v1/network/delegates` directly (easy grep: `v<uid>.djinn.gg/v1/network/delegates` appearing in `web/**/*.ts`).

### 2026-04-20 23:53 UTC — HIGH — UID 0 scoring loop stall confirmed: shares_held=1245 across 4 iters (~24h), version=1363+1 across 4 iters, 9 tags behind tip; supersedes iter-35 MED

- [x] (superseded by iter-38 CRITICAL diagnostic on 2026-04-20 01:08 UTC; `shares_held` was a misread) The "scoring loop stall" hypothesis collapses under live diagnostic: `shares_held` is literally `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM shares` — a DB row-count of stored Shamir key shares, NOT a scoring-activity counter. It stays flat when no new signals are committed, which matches zero SignalCommitted events observed on-chain in ~11h (`cast logs SignalCommitment --from-block -10000`). Meanwhile UID 0 v1378+1 reports `outcomes_resolved_total=2, last_outcome_resolved_ms=...` (4m old) — resolutions ARE happening. Validator is healthy; finding's premise was wrong. See iter-38 CRITICAL entry's close-out for the full diagnostic chain. Future scoring-activity checks should use `outcomes_resolved_total` (added in v1379), NOT `shares_held`.
- **Symptom:** `curl https://v0.djinn.gg/health` returns `"version": "1363+1", "shares_held": 1245, "pending_outcomes": 20` at iter-36 (2026-04-20 23:53 UTC). Both fields are now FROZEN across **4 consecutive iters** spanning **~24 hours** (iter-33 @ 23:56 UTC: 1245, iter-34 @ 22:19 UTC: 1245, iter-35 @ 22:53 UTC: 1245, iter-36 @ 23:53 UTC: 1245). Pending outcomes also flat at 20 across all 4. Pre-registered iter-35 hypothesis (2) ("scoring loop independently stuck — if iter-36 sees shares_held":1245 a 4th time, this hypothesis ranks first") is now the leading diagnosis.
- **Diagnosis (re-ranked from iter-35):** (1) **Most likely now — scoring loop genuinely stalled, NOT just operator-restart lag**: 4 iters × ~6h = ~24h of zero share growth on a validator with 20 pending outcomes is well outside normal noise. Validator is responding to /health (chain_connected, bt_connected, attest_capable all true) but its shares-held counter is frozen even as outcomes pile up unsettled. Suggests a defect in the share-accrual path (settlement listener missing events, scoring loop crashed silently inside an async task, or pm2's autorestart respawned the process but the long-running scoring goroutine didn't come back). (2) **Plausible — operator pulled v1372 between iter-35 and iter-36 but scoring loop hasn't reconverged**: if pm2 cycled, the loop might still be replaying historical outcomes; but `version` would have moved off 1363+1, which it hasn't, so this is unlikely. (3) **Edge case — UID 0 isolated by peer notary-sampling (v1169 same-coldkey exclusion)**: this should still produce *some* share movement from outcomes where it's not excluded, so it only explains a partial stall, not a complete one — does not match "0 movement across 24h."
- **Cross-check candidate (cheap, do this first):** v1's `shares_held=1077` at iter-36; iter-35 didn't capture v1's shares_held so we can't yet check v1's trajectory. Iter-37 will have the comparison data. **If v1 is *also* flat at 1077 across iters 36→37, the bug is in shared validator code post-v1362 (network-wide, every operator on pre-v1372 is affected)**. If v1 is moving but UID 0 isn't, the bug is UID 0-box-specific (env, OS state, or stuck async task). This single comparison is the cheapest disambiguation available; do it before any infrastructure intervention.
- **Repro:** `curl -s https://v0.djinn.gg/health | jq '{version,shares_held,pending_outcomes}'` → `{version:"1363+1", shares_held:1245, pending_outcomes:20}`. Cross-check across 4 consecutive iters (lines 24, 50 [iter-34 entry], and the iter-33 line below for shares_held=1245 history).
- **Triage hint:** Fix path bifurcates on diagnosis: (a) **Diagnosis (1) — scoring loop stall**: validator-code bug; the cross-check on v1 above tells you whether to file a P0 validator bug (network-wide) or whether UID 0's box needs targeted operator action. (b) **Diagnosis (2) — operator pull-and-restart**: cure is `ssh root@37.60.251.252 'cd /root/djinn && git pull && pnpm -C validator install && pm2 restart djinn-validator'`, but operator decisions are out-of-scope for `/djinn-e2e` — the right action is a Telegram nudge from `/djinn`. **Do NOT** SSH into the box from this skill. **Do NOT** open a PR against validator code without first confirming the bug reproduces on a non-UID-0 validator. The first triage action for `/djinn` is the iter-37 cross-check on v1's `shares_held`.
- **Severity rationale:** HIGH because (a) iter-35 pre-registered the threshold and it has been crossed cleanly (4 iters flat, ~24h, pending outcomes also flat — every measurable signal points to "not progressing"), (b) the scoring loop is *the* mechanism that translates validator work into emissions and burn-address allocation; if it's stuck, the burn-address (UID 0, per `feedback_uid0_burn.md`) is over-allocated emissions on outcomes it can't settle, distorting the emissions split for the rest of the network until restarted, (c) this is exactly the silent failure the World Cup load spike (54 days) would surface catastrophically — quietly stuck scoring loops at 10× traffic become incident-level outages. Not CRITICAL because the regression suite still passes end-to-end and no UI surface has broken; impact is on validator economics + settlement, not on the user-facing golden path. Will bump to CRITICAL at iter-37 if (a) shares_held still 1245 a 5th iter AND (b) v1's shares_held is also flat across 36→37 (network-wide validator bug, World-Cup-blocking).

Verification: once v0 deploys v1380, any future v189 slowness no longer gates the request — v213 or v2 will win the race in <2s regardless of v189's 11s.) Compare iter-35: 4 successful 200s @ 9–13s with a separate 3-burst of 500s. **The 500 pattern has shifted from clustered-then-recovered to interleaved-1:1 with 200s** — i.e., the endpoint is now intermittently failing rather than briefly degrading then bouncing back. 4 control validators on the same endpoint are healthy: v0=2.497s, v1=1.616s, v2=1.851s, v213=0.694s — v189 stays the slowest by 5–17×. Other v189 endpoints remain normal (`/health` 1.491s, `/v1/network/validators` 0.051s, `/v1/network/miners` 0.073s) — endpoint-specific to `/v1/idiot/browse`.
- **Diagnosis (ranked, refined from iter-35):** (1) **Most likely — endpoint-side query/scan bottleneck on v189's box at saturation**: latency pattern is endpoint-specific; the shift from clustered 500s to interleaved 500s suggests the endpoint is now *consistently* near its query-time-budget edge — fast enough to occasionally complete but slow enough that random load spikes tip it over into timeout. (2) **Possible — subgraph replica mirror lag has worsened**: v189 on `version":"1364"` is 8 tags behind tip; if v1365+ included a /v1/idiot/browse query optimisation, v189 lacks it. (3) **Operator-side**: nothing /djinn-e2e can do directly.
- **Repro:** `for i in 1 2 3 4; do curl -sS -o /dev/null -w "HTTP %{http_code} %{time_total}s\n" -m 20 https://v189.djinn.gg/v1/idiot/browse; done` reliably mixes 200@9–13s and 500@2–3s. Control: same loop on v0, v1, v2, v213 stays under 3s with no 500s.
- **Triage hint:** Two complementary fixes — do BOTH; (a) is a 1-line tactical mitigation, (b) is the structural fix the network needs anyway:
  - **(a) Quick mitigation** in `web/lib/api.ts`: shrink `BROWSER_BOOTSTRAP_HOSTS` from `[v0, v1, v2, v189, v213]` to `[v0, v1, v2, v213]` (drop v189). Cost: 1-line array edit; removes one fallback option but 4 healthy hosts is more than enough for bootstrap. Restores fast worst-case recovery — currently a v0+v1+v2 triple-outage forces an 8s timeout on v189 before reaching v213; with v189 dropped, fallback to v213 happens in ~1s.
  - **(b) Structural fix** in same file at `fetchFromAnyValidator` (around `web/lib/api.ts:351`): switch from sequential fallback to `Promise.any`-style race. Pattern: kick off all N requests simultaneously, return the first 2xx response, reject only if all reject. This generalises to N validators without linear timeout blowup — the right architecture for browser-direct CORS as the network grows. The network will have 30+ validators by World Cup; sequential fallback through a slow one would add 8s × 30 = 4 minutes worst-case. Trade-off: N concurrent requests is more bandwidth/CPU on the browser but trivial relative to the savings, and dovetails with the existing nginx wildcard router pattern (which already broadcasts). Extend `web/lib/__tests__/hooks.test.ts` with a "first-fast-wins" test.
- **Severity rationale:** HIGH because (a) iter-35 pre-registered criterion is met (sticky across 2 iters with *intensifying* 500 rate, not just stable), (b) the structural problem (sequential fallback through unknown-quality validators) gets *worse* as the network grows — fix it *before* World Cup adds 30+ validators to the bootstrap list, (c) actual user impact is currently small (v0 first and fast, most users never hit v189), but the failure mode is "if v0/v1/v2 all happen to be slow during a traffic spike, every browser stalls 8s on v189 before getting any data" — exactly the correlated-failure pattern that surfaces under load. Not CRITICAL because v0 alone serving the golden path makes today-impact small; making this CRITICAL would inflate severity for a known-structural issue. Will not auto-escalate further unless v0 also degrades, in which case the cascade matters.

### 2026-04-20 22:53 UTC — MED — v1372 browser-direct fan-out exposes v189 `/v1/idiot/browse` latency + intermittent 5xx: 9–13s (4 samples), HTTP 500 on 3 consecutive hits, breaches fetchFromAnyValidator 8s budget

- [x] (fixed in 5bef9847 via v1380 — superseded by the HIGH iter-36 finding above; structural fix (c2) implemented as called out in this MED's Triage hint.) Reported by `/djinn-e2e` heuristic (`curl` spot-check of the 5 `BROWSER_BOOTSTRAP_HOSTS` introduced in v1372's `web/lib/api.ts:323`). NEW surface this iter — before v1372 the browser never called v189 directly; after v1372 it's in the serial fallback chain.
- **Symptom:** `curl -m 20 https://v189.djinn.gg/v1/idiot/browse` measures **9.353s, 13.374s, 8.847s, 9.891s** across 4 successful 200-response samples (all returning 46-byte JSON, same payload shape as v0). An intermediate burst of 3 consecutive requests returned **HTTP 500** in 2.0–2.7s (`{"detail":"Internal server error"}`) before the endpoint self-recovered. The remaining 4 bootstrap validators {v0, v1, v2, v213} all respond **200 in 0.822–2.166s** for the same request — a **5–10× latency spread** and a clear outlier. Other v189 endpoints are fine: `/health` 1.491s, `/v1/network/validators` 0.051s, `/v1/network/miners` 0.073s. So this is endpoint-specific (browse only), not a validator-wide slowness.
- **Diagnosis (ranked):** (1) **Most likely — subgraph / DB scan not cached**: v189 is on `version":"1364"` (1 past v0's 1363+1, 8 behind v1372). A cache / index optimisation for `/v1/idiot/browse`'s 7-day scan may have landed in v1365+ that v189 doesn't have yet. The transient 500s (clustered, then recovered) look like query-timeout rejections under load. (2) **Also possible — subgraph mirror lag**: v189 may be reading from a slower / behind-head subgraph replica. Consistent with sporadic 500s under momentarily heavier read pressure. (3) **Unlikely — wildcard proxy**: `dig +short v189.djinn.gg` returns `37.60.251.252` (our UID 0 VPS wildcard router), but `curl http://161.97.150.248:8421/health` (v189's real IP per memory) returns 200 in 1.3s, so the wildcard is forwarding correctly. Latency is validator-compute, not proxy.
- **Repro:** `for i in 1 2 3 4; do curl -sS -o /dev/null -w "HTTP %{http_code} %{time_total}s\n" -m 20 https://v189.djinn.gg/v1/idiot/browse; done` — expect a mix of 200 @ 8–13s and occasional 500 @ 2–3s. Control: `curl -sS -o /dev/null -w "HTTP %{http_code} %{time_total}s\n" -m 10 https://v0.djinn.gg/v1/idiot/browse` consistently returns 200 in <2s across all samples this iter.
- **Triage hint:** (a) `web/lib/api.ts:351` `fetchFromAnyValidator` defaults `timeoutMs = 8_000`. v189 at 9–13s will **reliably hit the AbortSignal.timeout(8000)** — the helper throws, the outer try falls through to v213, which serves 200 in <1s. So the **user-visible impact is +8s latency IF v0/v1/v2 all fail first** (unlikely but the entire point of the fallback list). (b) Worth confirming via MCP-playwright from a real browser that CORS preflight (`OPTIONS`) doesn't add its own delay on v189 — the nginx wildcard should set `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` for djinn.gg, but a slow preflight would compound the timeout; static-curl tests can't probe this because they skip preflight. (c) Two reasonable fixes, both cheap: **(c1)** shrink `BROWSER_BOOTSTRAP_HOSTS` to `[v0, v1, v2, v213]` (drop v189) until operator investigates — 4 fast hosts is plenty for bootstrap; **(c2)** parallelize `fetchFromAnyValidator` via `Promise.any` over all 5 hosts so the slowest host never gates recovery (pattern: race all five, return first 2xx, reject only if all fail). (c2) is the structurally right fix because it generalizes to 10+ validators without a linear fallback-time blowup. (d) **Do NOT** try to "fix" v189 alone from this side — the endpoint-side slowness is an operator decision for that validator's box; filing here is to surface the architectural sensitivity, not to prescribe a per-validator patch.
- **Severity rationale:** MED because (a) v1372 is brand new (~26 min old at time of measurement) and this is the first sweep where the browser actually exercises v189 directly; a real user on a slow network could reproduce easily; (b) the sequential fallback means a slow validator in the bootstrap list adds 8s of wait per failed prior host — v189 being 4th of 5 means worst-case recovery from v0/v1/v2 outage is v189-timeout (8s) + v213 success (~1s) ≈ **9s instead of ~1s** if v189 were absent; (c) the 500s suggest v189 is under query-time pressure that the browse scan will reproduce at scale as more real users arrive; (d) not HIGH because v0 (first in order) is fast and covers the golden path — v189 only hurts users when v0/v1/v2 all fail simultaneously, which is rare. If iter-36 still sees v189 at 9+s on `/v1/idiot/browse`, bump to HIGH and action the `fetchFromAnyValidator` parallelisation (c2 above) because the problem is structural, not transient.
- **Screenshot:** n/a (HTTP-level, all probes via `curl`).

(fixed in v1302 · Layout.tsx:271 outer row `flex-wrap gap-y-2`, inner link container `flex-wrap gap-x-4 gap-y-2`)
- **Symptom:** At 375×812 iPhone viewport, `document.body.scrollWidth = 404px` (viewport 375 + allowed overflow 20 = 395 threshold; exceeds by 9px). Visible horizontal scrollbar at bottom of every page. Test error: `Expected: <= 395, Received: 404`.
- **Diagnosis:** `web/components/Layout.tsx:271-282` — the footer's bottom legal bar is a single `flex items-center justify-between` row with no `flex-wrap`. On the right side it packs `Support | Terms | Privacy | Risk | AUP | DMCA | Feedback` = 7 items with `gap-4` (16px each), which alone is ~270–360px of minimum content width. Combined with the `© 2026 Djinn Inc.` copyright on the left (~100px) the inner flex exceeds the 343px available content area inside `max-w-7xl px-4` on a 375px viewport, pushing `html.scrollWidth` past the 395 tolerance. Because this is in the shared `Layout.tsx` footer, **every route is affected**, not just `/genius` (the page the failing test happened to land on). The screenshot below captures it on `/genius` but the cause is global.
- **Suggested fix** (observer-only, not applying):
  - Easiest: add `flex-wrap gap-y-2` to the footer bottom-bar flex row at line 271 so the links wrap onto two lines on narrow viewports. Minimal visual churn, zero API changes.
  - Or: on mobile breakpoint (sm:), stack the copyright above the links (`flex-col sm:flex-row`) and let the link group wrap internally.
  - Or: demote Feedback to an icon-button on mobile since the other 6 are route links and Feedback is the outlier (opens a modal, not a page).
- **Repro:** `cd ~/djinn/web && set -a && source .env.local && set +a && xvfb-run npx playwright test --config playwright.ux.config.ts --grep "mobile viewport"`. Consistent fail (not flaky) — the overflow is deterministic CSS, not a timing issue.
- **Triage hint:** Don't chase this inside `/genius/page.tsx` (which is what the test navigated to) — the overflow comes from the shared `Layout.tsx` footer, so the repro will show up on any route if you resize to 375. Also, the prior iter-11 run was clean at this test on the same config, so you can `git blame web/components/Layout.tsx 271` and confirm `6a7777b` is the regressor. The 3 new legal routes (`/risk`, `/acceptable-use`, `/dmca`) are valuable — don't remove the links, just wrap them.
- **Severity rationale:** HIGH because (a) affects every page, (b) World Cup countdown is 54 days out and first-time mobile users hitting horizontal scroll on any page degrades trust, (c) cheap fix (one className change).
- **Screenshot:** `e2e-screenshots/2026-04-18-mobile-overflow.png`

At current rate (~0.00001 per 5 iters, iter cron is every ~3h), a meaningful drain is probably 20–30 more iters away — but ahead of a World Cup load spike (52 days out), we should not start a 10× traffic week with a dry deployer.
- **Repro:** `cd /home/user/djinn/web && set -a && source .env.local && set +a && node -e "const { ethers } = require('ethers'); const p = new ethers.JsonRpcProvider('https://sepolia.base.org'); const w = new ethers.Wallet(process.env.E2E_DEPLOYER_KEY, p); p.getBalance(w.address).then(b => console.log(ethers.formatEther(b)));"` — prints balance.
- **Triage hint:** faucet top-up from `https://www.alchemy.com/faucets/base-sepolia` or `https://thirdweb.com/base-sepolia/faucet` to deployer address; 0.05 ETH from faucet gives ~100 iters of headroom. Not urgent (not blocking today's run), but should be scheduled before the Cup. Not a product bug — operational hygiene.
- **Severity rationale:** MED — below the skill's documented threshold, but not yet preventing test runs (this iter's skip of genius/purchase was caused by env-load miss, not balance). Flagging now so /djinn loop or operator can action it cheaply rather than discovering it mid-incident during the Cup.

### 2026-04-19 02:55 UTC — LOW — Visitor journey skips `/attest` even though it sits in the top nav between `/network` and `/docs`; P1-11 attestation surface has no UI regression coverage

- [x] Reported by `/djinn-e2e` heuristic (source spot-check) — coverage-expansion request, not a product defect. (fixed in v1314 · `web/e2e/ux/journeys/visitor.spec.ts:100-105` — Step 5.5 inserts `clickNav(page, "Attest")` → URL assertion → `humanScroll` → `humanDelay`, exactly the 4-line shape the triage hint recommended. Typecheck clean. Will be exercised by the next `/djinn-e2e` sweep.)
- **Symptom:** `web/components/Layout.tsx:18` lists `/attest` as the 6th item in the primary `NAV_LINKS` array (Home → Genius → Idiot → Leaderboard → Network → **Attest** → Docs → About). It also appears in the footer's "Network" column at `Layout.tsx:265`. But `web/e2e/ux/journeys/visitor.spec.ts:25-117` walks Home → Genius → Idiot → Leaderboard → Network → Docs → About → Home, **skipping `/attest` entirely**. So a regression on `/attest` (404, JS error, broken form, slow first paint) would not be caught by `/djinn-e2e` until a user reports it.
- **Diagnosis:** The `/attest` page is the user-facing surface for P1-11 (TLSNotary attestation flow) — `web/app/attest/page.tsx` is a real, polished React client component with status states (`idle`/`proving`/`verifying`/`done`/`error`), validator-vs-client verification distinction, custom error humanization (`humanizeAttestError`), and timing metadata. It was the iter-18 next-iter watch item ("any P1-11 attestation UI") and as of HEAD `c624543` the surface exists but is not exercised. The visitor journey was last edited before `/attest` was promoted to top-nav, so this is a stale gap rather than an intentional omission.
- **Suggested fix** (observer-only, not applying): in `visitor.spec.ts` between Step 5 (Network) and Step 6 (Docs), insert a step that does `await clickNav(page, "Attest"); await expect(page).toHaveURL(/\/attest/, { timeout: 10_000 }); await expect(page.locator("h1, h2").first()).toBeVisible({ timeout: 10_000 }); await humanDelay(page);`. Cost: 4 lines, ~1s of additional test runtime. No on-chain calls needed; the page renders without a wallet. This brings visitor-journey coverage in line with the actual top-nav surface area.
- **Repro:** `grep -nE "goto|clickNav|attest" web/e2e/ux/journeys/visitor.spec.ts` returns no `attest` matches; `grep -n attest web/components/Layout.tsx` confirms it's in `NAV_LINKS`.
- **Triage hint:** Don't add a separate `attest.spec.ts` — the page is anonymous-readable (you only need a wallet to actually run an attestation, not to land on the page), so it belongs in `visitor.spec.ts` alongside the other read-only nav stops. If you want deeper coverage later (actually submit a URL and assert the proof state), do that in a follow-up `attest.spec.ts`. For now, the 4-line nav add is the minimum-viable regression.
- **Severity rationale:** LOW — coverage gap, not a defect. `/attest` itself appears to render correctly (the working tree has Mar-22 manual screenshots showing successful proofs, and the page hasn't changed in `git log -- web/app/attest/`). The risk this finding mitigates is *future* regression, not current breakage. World Cup countdown (53 days) means coverage gaps that don't bite today still matter — but this is the lowest tier.
