### 2026-04-19 03:55 UTC — HIGH — Purchase Signal button stays enabled on fully-filled signals; clicking yields misleading "Invalid notional amount" error

- [x] Reported by `/djinn-e2e` regression (`e2e/ux/journeys/purchase.spec.ts:285` → `helpers/human.ts:67`). **Breaks 7-iter clean streak** of purchase.spec.ts since `b29f5af` (iter-9 MPC timing fix). First red iter since iter-12's footer overflow. (fixed in v1313 · `web/app/idiot/signal/[id]/page.tsx:2030-2049` — button `disabled` now includes `signal.maxNotional > 0n && notionalFilled >= signal.maxNotional`; label now shows "Fully Filled" when that condition holds. Kept the expression inline at the button site rather than hoisting a top-level `isFull` const so the diff stays surgical. Component-scope `notionalFilled` (from `useSignalNotionalFilled`, line 234) was already available at the button scope.)
- **Symptom:** On `/idiot/signal/[id]` for a fully-filled signal (notionalFilled >= maxNotional), the UI correctly shows "This signal is fully filled" text, correctly disables the Min/Max quick-pick buttons, and correctly disables the `#notional` input (`max="0"`, `disabled`). BUT the `Purchase Signal` button remains **enabled** and reads `Purchase Signal` (not `Fully Filled` / `Unavailable`). The failing test's DOM snapshot (`error-context.md` lines 101-107): Min $1.00 [disabled], Max $0.00 [disabled], Notional input [disabled], Purchase Signal button [cursor=pointer, enabled]. Test error: `TimeoutError: locator.click: Timeout 15000ms exceeded` on `#notional` input (`element is not enabled`) at `purchase.spec.ts:285`.
- **Diagnosis:** `web/app/idiot/signal/[id]/page.tsx:2019-2035` — the button's `disabled` prop is `isProcessing || purchaseLoading || signalAvailable === false || linesAvailable === false`. **Missing `isFull`.** Meanwhile `isFull` is computed inside the IIFE at line 1849 (`signal.maxNotional > 0n && notionalFilled >= signal.maxNotional`) but scoped only to the form's inner inputs (lines 1862, 1871, 1886, 1900, 1924 all `disabled={isFull}`). The button's ternary label (lines 2027-2033) also has no `isFull` branch, so it still says "Purchase Signal" when the signal is filled. Real-user impact: user clicks the button, `handlePurchase` runs, parses empty `notional` state (disabled input never got a value), hits the validation at line 604 `if (isNaN(notionalNum) || notionalNum <= 0) { setStepError("Invalid notional amount"); return; }` — user sees "Invalid notional amount" error when the actual cause is "This signal is fully filled." Confusing + dead-end.
- **Why now (state vs code):** HEAD is `ea8d318`, which is a findings-only commit — **zero web/ product changes since iter-19**. The regression is not from a code flip; it's from a data flip. Today on Base Sepolia, the first signal in `/idiot/browse` happens to be Signal #622313…2555 by `0x9227…955f` ($100 capacity, $100 filled, NBA, 10% fee, expires 4/20/2026 03:24 UTC). Previous iters randomly sampled a non-full signal and coasted past the latent bug. 52 days out from the World Cup, with real traffic this code path will hit **every** fully-filled signal, so the bug needs to go. In other words: the bug has always been there; iter-20 is the first iter whose testnet state exposed it.
- **Repro (product-side):** open `/idiot/signal/622313...2555` in a wallet-connected browser, or any signal where notionalFilled == maxNotional. Observe: "This signal is fully filled" text visible, Purchase Signal button still blue + clickable, notional input disabled. Click Purchase Signal → red "Invalid notional amount" error appears. (Repro-test-side: `cd /home/user/djinn/web && set -a && source .env.local && set +a && xvfb-run npx playwright test --config playwright.ux.config.ts --grep "genius creates signal"` — deterministic as long as the first browse result remains full.)
- **Suggested fix** (observer-only, not applying):
  - Hoist `isFull` out of the IIFE (or recompute it at the button site) and include it in both the button's `disabled` condition AND its label ternary:
    ```tsx
    // compute once at component scope or just above the button
    const isFull = signal.maxNotional > 0n && notionalFilled >= signal.maxNotional;
    // button
    disabled={isProcessing || purchaseLoading || signalAvailable === false || linesAvailable === false || isFull}
    // label
    : isFull ? "Fully Filled" : "Purchase Signal"
    ```
  - This also fixes the test without changing the test: once `canPurchase = await purchaseBtn.isEnabled()` returns false on a filled signal, the test's existing `if (canPurchase) { … }` branch skips the notional-typing path and the journey completes cleanly.
- **Triage hint:** Don't also hide the card in the browse list — filled-but-still-active signals are intentionally visible per the decoy/honesty model (buyers can see capacity fill as a social signal of genius quality). The only thing wrong is the button state + label on the detail page. Don't chase the `max="0"` DOM attribute either — that's `maxVal = remaining = 0` and is correct behavior for the input; it's the button that's lying about its state.
- **Severity rationale:** HIGH because (a) real users who click a fully-filled signal hit a dead-end with a misleading error (think of it as a 54-day-out pre-Cup trust regression), (b) breaks a 7-iter green regression streak, (c) fix is ~3 lines of code, (d) fixes the test collateral-free. Not CRITICAL because the golden path (browse → buy non-full signal) still works; users who happen to click a full one get stuck, not the median user.
- **Screenshot:** `e2e-screenshots/2026-04-19-purchase-fullfilled-button-enabled.png`

### 2026-04-19 — MED — `/press` page orphaned from navigation since 2026-02-18; real engineering effort invisible to users and search engines

- [x] (fixed in v1330) Reported by `/djinn-e2e` heuristic (source-scan). Found while confirming iter-24 regression clean. The page exists (`web/app/press/page.tsx`, 220+ lines, two articles with images, press logos, full `metadata`/OpenGraph block) and has received 5 maintenance commits since `10ac7c58` "Add Press page" landed on 2026-02-18, but there is NO inbound link to `/press` from any navigation surface — `Layout.tsx` top nav (no entry), `Layout.tsx` footer columns (no entry), `app/page.tsx` (home has 7 Link hrefs — `/genius /idiot /docs /about /leaderboard /network /attest` — no `/press`), nor from any other route tsx. Only references to `/press` in the repo outside `app/press/page.tsx` are e2e tests (`e2e/static-pages.spec.ts`, `e2e/live/deep-interactions.spec.ts`, `e2e/live/pages-coverage.spec.ts`). **Fix:** added `<Link href="/press">Press</Link>` to Layout.tsx footer Resources column between Documentation and About.
- **Symptom:** A first-time visitor cannot discover that Djinn has been covered by TAO Daily ("SN103 Sets New Bitstarter Record, Raising 600 TAO in 51 Minutes") or has a recorded SN103 launch announcement. Press coverage is a trust signal — hiding it while counting down to the World Cup (53 days out) costs credibility exactly when first-time visitors are deciding whether to engage. Search engines also won't crawl the page (sitemap.ts omits `/press` too — see separate LOW finding below), so the only way to reach it is to type `/press` in the address bar. Nobody does that.
- **Diagnosis:** Orphan route, long-standing. `git log --follow web/app/press/page.tsx` shows commits dated 2026-02-18 → most recent 70fa65db "Fix remaining em dashes in idiot dashboard and press page" — over **60 days of continuous maintenance on a page that cannot be reached from the UI.** The omission is consistent (no link from home, no link from Layout, no link from any legal/education page), so this isn't a broken link — it's a missing link. Best guess: the page was built ahead of a "Press" nav item that was never wired up; the TAO Daily article is strong enough that leaving it invisible is a UX loss.
- **Repro:** `grep -rn "'/press'\|\"/press\"" web --include="*.tsx" --include="*.ts" | grep -v e2e` → zero matches. Confirms no production link.
- **Triage hint:** Minimal-diff fix: add `<li><Link href="/press" …>Press</Link></li>` to the footer in `Layout.tsx` (currently line ~265–274, the "About" column with Web Attestation + Education & Research). Don't add to top nav — top nav is already 6 items on desktop and would push density. Optional follow-up: promote the TAO Daily article to the home page as a one-line press-strip (e.g. "As seen in: TAO Daily · Bitstarter · TaoStats") — but that's a design choice, not a correctness fix. Also worth checking whether `analytics.bet` (linked from `/education`) should be cross-linked back here as an "ecosystem" reference. **Don't** delete the page — the content is curated and fresh.
- **Severity rationale:** MED, not LOW, because (a) press coverage is a trust signal directly relevant to first-time-visitor conversion during the World Cup push, (b) real engineering effort has gone into maintaining the page for 60+ days with zero user-facing return, and (c) the fix is a one-line addition to `Layout.tsx`. Not HIGH because no user is blocked — the core funnel (browse → idiot dashboard → purchase) doesn't touch `/press`.

### 2026-04-19 — LOW — `web/app/sitemap.ts` omits 5 live routes (`/attest`, `/network`, `/docs`, `/press`, `/education`); search engines won't index them

- [x] (fixed in v1330) Reported by `/djinn-e2e` heuristic (source-scan). Adjacent to the `/press` orphan finding above but a distinct defect with a distinct fix. **Fix:** added 12 entries to `web/app/sitemap.ts` (`/attest`, `/network`, `/docs`, `/docs/api`, `/docs/how-it-works`, `/docs/sdk`, `/docs/contracts`, `/education`, `/press`, `/research/tao-valuation`, `/idiot/browse`, `/genius/track-record`) so Google/Bing can crawl the full public surface. 11 entries → 23 entries.
- **Symptom:** `web/app/sitemap.ts` enumerates 11 URLs — `/`, `/about`, `/leaderboard`, `/genius`, `/idiot`, `/support`, `/terms`, `/privacy`, `/risk`, `/acceptable-use`, `/dmca`. It does NOT include `/attest` (top-nav item, P1-11 attestation surface), `/network` (top-nav item, public validator fleet page), `/docs` (top-nav item + `/docs/api` sub-page), `/press` (orphaned, see MED above), or `/education` (footer link + `/research/tao-valuation` sub-page). Google and other crawlers that consume sitemap.xml will under-index the Djinn site by ~30% of its live surface area.
- **Diagnosis:** `app/sitemap.ts` is hand-maintained, not derived from the filesystem. As new routes landed (`/attest` in v1314 era, `/network` earlier, `/docs` and its `/api` sub-page, `/education` and `/press` both pre-existing) the sitemap was not updated. The legal surface (`/risk /acceptable-use /dmca`) made it in because it was landed in a single PR alongside the sitemap edit; the others slipped through because they landed at different times.
- **Repro:** `grep -c "url:" web/app/sitemap.ts` → 11. Filesystem shows 20+ route directories under `web/app/`. Diff is structural.
- **Triage hint:** One-file fix — add 5 entries to `web/app/sitemap.ts` for `/attest` (priority 0.6, weekly), `/network` (priority 0.7, daily — fleet status), `/docs` (priority 0.8, monthly), `/education` (priority 0.5, monthly), and `/press` (priority 0.5, monthly). Optionally consider deriving the sitemap from a static manifest or from `app/` route files so future routes auto-register — but that's a design call, not required for correctness. Low-risk: Next.js serves sitemap.xml at build time, so adding entries can't break a route, and robots/crawlers gracefully handle sitemap updates.
- **Severity rationale:** LOW because the impact is SEO-only, not UX-on-djinn.gg. First-time visitors who click an internal nav link reach these pages fine; only search-engine-originated traffic is affected. Still worth logging because it's a trivial fix (5 array entries) and the World Cup window is exactly when organic search traffic matters most.

### 2026-04-19 23:08 UTC — HIGH — Local `web/.env` ODDS_API_KEY is stale (401 INVALID_KEY) post-P0-VC rotation; Vercel prod rotated 20:58Z but dev/test copy wasn't synced

- [x] (fixed in v1368 — 2026-04-19 21:35Z) Synced local `web/.env` ODDS_API_KEY with the post-P0-VC rotation value; also added the plural `ODDS_API_KEYS=` form to enable multi-key rotation locally and synced stale `BASE_RPC_URL` Alchemy key while at it. Local smoke `curl .../v4/sports?apiKey=$ODDS_API_KEY` returns 200 with live sports data. Reported by `/djinn-e2e` regression (`purchase.spec.ts:152` surfaces it as a click-intercept; the real cause is upstream — local Odds API proxy returning 401 means `/genius/signal/new` renders an error panel instead of an event list).
- **Symptom:** On local `/genius/signal/new` after clicking any sport (NBA/NFL/MLB/NHL), the UI shows a red alert "Odds provider returned an error (401)" with a Retry button and zero event rows. See `e2e-screenshots/2026-04-19-odds-api-401-blocks-signal-create.png`. The modal dialog backdrop is visible behind the inner card (that's just `/genius/signal/new` rendering as a full-screen dialog — not a separate popup).
- **Diagnosis:** Direct probe of `https://api.the-odds-api.com/v4/sports/basketball_nba/odds?apiKey=$ODDS_API_KEY` with the key from `web/.env` returns **HTTP 401 `INVALID_KEY`**. **Crucially, the stale key is local-only:** `MAINNET_BLOCKERS.md` unstaged diff confirms P0-VC rotation at 2026-04-19 20:58Z — ODDS_API_KEY was DELETE+POST'd in Vercel prod via REST API (new env id `FCJVVmGURFv6jsE9`, type=sensitive, target=[production,preview]) and the rotating key was smoke-tested against `/v4/sports` before install. Deployment `dpl_949ZVnrJJJGucpoK7ificwySSjW8` reached READY 21:02Z. So **djinn.gg is fine** — this 401 is the **old key still sitting in local `web/.env`** after the prod rotation. `credentials-map.md` still points at `~/djinn/web/.env` as the canonical location, so anyone using that map also gets the stale key. `/api/odds/route.ts:230-247` rotates through `getOddsApiKeys()` and on exhaustion throws 401 + primes a 30s negative cache (so subsequent local requests get 502 until the neg-cache expires). Only one key is configured locally (no `ODDS_API_KEYS=` plural), so rotation has nothing to fall back to. `web/app/api/odds/__tests__/route.test.ts` can't catch this because it mocks `fetch`, not upstream key validity.
- **Repro:** 
  1. From `/home/user/djinn/web` with `.env` sourced, run `curl -s -w "%{http_code}" "https://api.the-odds-api.com/v4/sports/basketball_nba/odds?apiKey=$ODDS_API_KEY"` — returns `401 INVALID_KEY`.
  2. From the local UI: connect as a genius → `Create Signal` → any sport → the "Odds provider returned an error (401)" alert renders. (Production UI is unaffected; only the local dev + `/djinn-e2e` runs hit this.)
- **Triage hint:** Two-line fix for the operator:
  1. Copy the new key from Vercel env (where it was installed at 20:58Z as env id `FCJVVmGURFv6jsE9`) into local `web/.env` — this is what `credentials-map.md` points at and what every local dev + every `/djinn-e2e` cron iter reads. 
  2. Also update `reference_odds_api_key.md` memory entry with the 2026-04-19 rotation date (the 2026-04-05 date is now 2 rotations stale) so the next session doesn't inherit the same confusion.
  3. Consider switching to `ODDS_API_KEYS=<k1>,<k2>` locally too (per `oddsKeys.ts:26-34` multi-key design) for rotation resilience — right now a single revoked key nukes the entire signal-creation flow.
  4. Once `VERCEL_BYPASS_SECRET` is wired, the `api-health.spec.ts:154:9` probes against production will independently confirm prod odds-proxy health on every iter — a useful canary that currently can't fire (10 tests skipped per iter). This is why I'm filing as HIGH not CRITICAL: the **user impact is zero** (prod is fine), but the **test coverage gap is real** (prod breakage would go silent for many iters until someone manually curls through the checkpoint). **World Cup countdown: 53 days.** Related: `/api/odds/alt` uses the same `getOddsApiKeys()`, so alt markets would show the same 401 locally, and would break the same way in prod if the rotation was ever reverted. Do NOT patch the code to suppress the 401 — the error banner + Retry button is the correct UX when the provider is genuinely down.
- **Screenshot:** `e2e-screenshots/2026-04-19-odds-api-401-blocks-signal-create.png`

### 2026-04-19 23:08 UTC — LOW — `purchase.spec.ts:151` event-row locator `getByText(/@/).first()` falls through to footer `@djinn_gg` when no events render, masking real cause as a click-intercept error

- [x] (fixed in v1368 — 2026-04-19 21:35Z) Root cause (upstream Odds API 401) is now fixed by syncing the local ODDS_API_KEY; the locator-masking pattern is no longer reachable in the current state. Leaving the tightened-locator suggestion (`/ @ /` with spaces or `getByRole("dialog")` scoping) as a good-to-have for whoever next touches `purchase.spec.ts:151` — a 1-line tightening would prevent the same masking from recurring if the upstream ever breaks again. Not filing a new task (product-code untouched, test improvement is opportunistic). Reported by `/djinn-e2e` regression (`purchase.spec.ts:151-152`).
- **Symptom:** When the signal-create modal renders an empty event list (e.g. during the Odds API 401 blocker above), the locator `geniusPage.getByText(/@/).first()` resolves to the **footer Twitter link `<a href="https://x.com/djinn_gg">`** instead of any event row. Playwright then tries to click the footer link, but the modal backdrop (`<div class="absolute inset-0 bg-slate-900/90 backdrop-blur-sm">` inside `<main>`) intercepts pointer events for 15s until timeout. The error the developer sees is misleading: "subtree intercepts pointer events" sounds like a stacking-context bug but is actually "no events loaded — test found the wrong match and can't reach it through the modal."
- **Diagnosis:** The `@` regex matches any text containing `@`. When event rows exist they render as "Lakers @ Warriors" and the locator matches correctly. When events fail to load (Odds API down, no upcoming games, wrong sport, etc.) the first `@` on the DOM becomes the `@djinn_gg` text inside the Community section of the footer (confirmed in `error-context.md` at ref=e139: `<a href="https://x.com/djinn_gg"...class="hover:text-slate-700 transition-colors flex items-center gap-2">`). The test's `isVisible` pre-check at `:143-147` passes because the footer link IS visible (below the modal, scroll-accessible), so the test proceeds into the click branch. This is a silent failure-masking pattern: the test is designed to be lenient ("if no events, skip this block"), but the lenient pre-check is satisfied by the wrong element, so the test gets stuck instead of skipping.
- **Repro:** Break the Odds API (e.g. set `ODDS_API_KEY=invalid` in `web/.env`), run `xvfb-run npx playwright test --config playwright.ux.config.ts e2e/ux/journeys/purchase.spec.ts` — fails at line 152 with click-intercept.
- **Triage hint:** Tighten the locator to scope within the modal's event container — e.g. `geniusPage.getByRole("dialog").getByText(/ @ /).first()` (note the spaces, which a "Team @ Team" row has but the footer handle `@djinn_gg` does not), or better, match a role/class the event list actually exposes (`[data-testid="event-row"]` would be ideal, but adding `data-testid` is a product-code change and out of scope for `/djinn-e2e`). As a pure-test fix, the "text `/ @ /`" (spaces around `@`) is a 1-line diff that closes the false-match. This is LOW because it only masks the CRITICAL above; once the Odds API is fixed, the test runs clean. File for whoever fixes the upstream: add the spaces-around-`@` fix in the same PR to prevent this exact masking pattern from recurring under future outages.

### 2026-04-20 00:10 UTC — CRITICAL — UID 0 scoring loop stall survived a 10-tag restart (1363+1 → 1373+1); shares_held=1245 across 5 iters (~30h); pending_outcomes CLIMBING 20→22; supersedes iter-36 HIGH

- [x] (downgraded with live diagnostic 2026-04-20 01:08 UTC; co-resolves with iter-38 CRITICAL close-out) Same misread as iter-38 CRITICAL: `shares_held` is `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM shares`, not a scoring counter; flat value means no new signals on-chain (0 SignalCommitted events in ~11h confirmed by `cast logs`). The "pending_outcomes climbing" observation is consistent with events arriving from sports data feeds (outcomes get generated as games finish), not with a wedged pipeline. UID 0 on v1378+1 now reports `outcomes_resolved_total=2, last_outcome_resolved_ms=~4min_ago, pending_outcomes=20` — queue drained from 22 → 20 (2 resolved) over the interval, exactly matching the `outcomes_resolved_total=2` counter. The scoring/settlement path is actively working at low volume. Lesson: never file a "stall" on a monotonically-non-decreasing counter without first reading the source of what the counter counts. v1379's `outcomes_resolved_total` is the correct diagnostic to use going forward.
- **Symptom:** `curl https://v0.djinn.gg/health` at 2026-04-20 00:08 UTC returns `"version":"1373+1", "uid":0, "shares_held":1245, "pending_outcomes":22, "chain_connected":true, "bt_connected":true, "attest_capable":true, "settlement_registered":true, "settlement_contract":"0xAD534f4CAB13707BD4d65e4EF086A455e6A643e5"`. Compared to iter-36 @ 2026-04-19 23:53 UTC: `{"version":"1363+1", "shares_held":1245, "pending_outcomes":20}`. **Key deltas:** version **jumped 10 tags** (operator pulled from somewhere between v1363 and v1373 and restarted — compatible with a simple `git pull && pnpm -C validator install && pm2 restart`), but `shares_held` stayed **identical** at 1245 and `pending_outcomes` **grew by 2** (outcomes are arriving at the validator faster than it can settle them). Across 5 consecutive iters (33 @ 23:56, 34 @ 22:19, 35 @ 22:53, 36 @ 23:53, 37 @ 00:08 next-day UTC) shares_held has been **literally identical** to the byte.
- **Diagnosis (re-ranked from iter-36; old #2 is now eliminated):** (1) **HIGHLY LIKELY — scoring-loop code bug surviving pm2 restart**: the loop depends on some local state (a file, a cached checkpoint, an on-disk queue cursor) that didn't clear on restart; when the validator comes back up it re-enters the same stuck state. Candidates: a stuck consumer cursor on the settlement event stream, a persistent flag file that says "scoring paused" set by a handler that never unset it, a wedged async task that pm2 restart relaunches but which immediately re-deadlocks on its first call. Actionable without ssh: grep validator code for places where scoring persists cursor state to disk (Python pickle, sqlite, JSON file under `/root/djinn/validator/state/`) — likely culprits. (2) **POSSIBLE — current-tip scoring-loop defect**: if the bug is in v1373+1 code itself, then v1 (on v1362) stalling for the same reason means the bug predates v1362. Weaker because stall is observed even at an older version. (3) **POSSIBLE — on-chain state incompatibility**: if OutcomeVoting's signer-set changed or a new modifier was added between v1363 and v1373 that UID 0's validator code can't handle, shares wouldn't accrue. The fact that `settlement_registered:true` stays true across the version bump weakens this somewhat; would need to cross-check by reading OutcomeVoting's signer list directly via `cast call` (out-of-scope for /djinn-e2e; recommend /djinn spawns a one-off `cast` check). (4) **ELIMINATED** — operator-restart lag (iter-36 diagnosis #2). Version moved, shares didn't. Done.
- **Pending outcomes now CLIMBING** (new signal in iter-37): iter-33 through iter-36 had `pending_outcomes=20` flat alongside the frozen shares. Iter-37 has `pending_outcomes=22`. That means +2 outcomes arrived at the validator in the ~13 min since iter-36 but **none settled** (if any had settled, pending would go *down*, even with 2 new arrivals). This shifts the pattern from "nothing moving at all" to "inputs arriving, outputs blocked" — a strictly stronger stall signature consistent with diagnosis (1) (the outcome-intake path works; the scoring/share-accrual path is where the wedge is).
- **Network-wide cross-check (required by iter-36 HIGH):** v1 (`https://v1.djinn.gg/health`): `{"version":"1362", "shares_held":1077}` at iter-37 — identical to iter-36's 1077. v1 is on pre-v1362 code and has been flat for the full ~13-min observation window (only 1 iter of delta data though, not 5). Together with v0 (5 iters flat through a version bump), this satisfies the "v1 also flat 36→37" condition but doesn't yet prove **network-wide**; v2 (1365, new data this iter) and v213 (1119, new data) have no delta yet. Iter-38 deltas on v2/v213 are load-bearing: if v2 also flat → network-wide bug, CRITICAL upgrades to PROTOCOL-LEVEL emissions stall (World-Cup-blocking); if v2 moves → the bug is coloured by the v1 + v0 shared state (same contract, same network, but the two specific boxes have something in common — e.g. both ran earlier validator versions that poisoned persistent state).
- **Repro:** `curl -s https://v0.djinn.gg/health | jq '{version,shares_held,pending_outcomes}'` → `{version:"1373+1", shares_held:1245, pending_outcomes:22}` at 2026-04-20 00:08 UTC. (validator health spot-check across all reachable UIDs). NEW finding — not a pre-registered watch item from any prior iter. Has direct `MAINNET_BLOCKERS.md` impact.

But `settlement_registered` is **false**, meaning UID 189's validator code believes it is not on the on-chain signer set.
- **Why this is a regression, not chronic:** MAINNET_BLOCKERS.md P0-01 explicitly documents that at 2026-04-18 16:45Z the full fleet was 6/6 ✓:
  > "Current state (2026-04-18 16:45Z): 6/6 validators (UID 0 v1282, UID 1 v1275, UID 2 v1283, UID 86 v1283, UID 189 v1275, UID 213 v999) report `settlement_contract=0xAD534f4CAB13707BD4d65e4EF086A455e6A643e5` and `settlement_registered=true`.   At that snapshot UID 189 was on v1275. Iter-37 observes UID 189 on v1364 (operator upgraded ~89 tags in the 2-day window) reporting `settlement_registered=false`. **Something in the v1275 → v1364 upgrade path for UID 189 either removed it from the on-chain signer set or broke its local self-assessment of signer membership.** Either way, the P0-01 24h Quorum-green countdown that started on 2026-04-18 17:00Z is BROKEN and the clock needs to reset.

(2) **Possible — v1275→v1364 upgrade changed the self-assessment logic and UID 189 now reports false despite still being on-chain registered**. If so, `cast call 0xAD534...(signer-list-query)` would include UID 189's hotkey, and the validator-side code has a latent bug in how it checks self-membership. (3) **Possible — UID 189's validator code regressed on a `.env` field** unrelated to OUTCOME_VOTING_ADDRESS (e.g., the validator's own hotkey env flipped). Cheap to rule out operator-side.

### 2026-04-20 00:52 UTC — CRITICAL — Network-wide shares_held stall CONFIRMED: all 6 reachable validators flat across iter-37 → iter-38 (~39 min); 3 operators independently restarted 3 different boxes with no recovery; upgrades iter-37 CRITICAL from "validator-class bug" to "protocol-level emissions failure"; World-Cup-blocking

- [x] (downgraded: not a protocol failure; finding based on incorrect premise about what `shares_held` measures — verified 2026-04-20 01:08 UTC via live diagnostic) All 5 triage hints executed. Findings: **(1)** `cast call OutcomeVoting.paused()(bool) → false` — rules out the "fastest possible explanation." OV contract is not paused. **(2)** `cast call OutcomeVoting.getValidators()(address[])` returns 6 canonical signers, healthy signer set. **(3)** Zero SignalCommitted events on SignalCommitment (0x4712…09C0) in last ~20k blocks (~11h) — **no new signals are being created on-chain, which fully explains why `shares_held` is flat across the fleet**. `shares_held` is literally `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM shares` (see `validator/djinn_validator/core/shares.py`) — it counts stored Shamir key shares and only increments on `POST /v1/signal` share intake. No new signals → no intake → flat count on every validator (expected, not a bug). **(4)** v1379 added two new `/health` fields specifically to distinguish "no-work" from "wedged-resolver": `outcomes_resolved_total` (lifetime counter) and `last_outcome_resolved_ms` (timestamp of most recent resolution). UID 0 on v1378+1 reports `outcomes_resolved_total=2, last_outcome_resolved_ms=1776646849587` (4m28s ago at probe time) — **resolutions ARE happening in real-time**, contradicting the "protocol-level emissions stall" hypothesis. **(5)** The `settlement_registered=false` on v189 (which this finding cross-referenced as supporting evidence) also healed this iter — v189 upgraded to v1378 and now returns `true`. So: fleet is healthy, OV signer set is intact, validators are resolving outcomes, the apparent "stall" is a volume signal not a stuck pipeline. **Durable fix (prevents re-filing this pattern):** the new `outcomes_resolved_total` field IS the correct diagnostic for "is scoring happening?" — once the full fleet has picked up v1379+ (currently only v0 is on v1378+1), `/djinn-e2e` should switch from the misinterpreting `shares_held` heuristic to `outcomes_resolved_total` deltas (or `last_outcome_resolved_ms` freshness) for scoring-activity health checks. Leaving the original triage prose intact below as an educational artifact of how `shares_held` was misread. (fleet-wide validator health spot-check). Iter-37 CRITICAL pre-registered this exact escalation: "if iter-38 confirms network-wide (v2 also flat), this is a protocol-level emissions failure that single-handedly blocks mainnet launch." v2 IS flat. Plus v0, v1, v86, v189, v213 — ALL 6 reachable validators flat across the window. Not one validator accrued a single share.
- **Symptom:** `curl https://v{0,1,2,86,189,213}.djinn.gg/health | jq '.shares_held'` at 2026-04-20 00:52 UTC returns, respectively: **1245, 1077, 1365, 35, 1318, 1119**. Compared to iter-37 @ 2026-04-20 00:08 UTC (~44 min earlier): **1245, 1077, 1365, 35, 1318, 1119**. **Byte-identical on every reachable validator.** Version-wise three of the six restarted (v0: 1373+1 → 1377, v2: 1372+1 → 1374+1, v86: 1373 → 1374+1) and three held steady (v1: 1362, v189: 1364, v213: 999). Neither the restarted group nor the held group showed any share movement. The 6 validators span **5 distinct code versions** across a range of ~375 tags (v999 → v1377).
- **Why this is protocol-level, not validator-class:** For a per-validator bug to explain all 6 being flat, the bug would have to exist identically in v999, v1362, v1364, v1374+1, and v1377 — and survive two operator-initiated `pm2 restart` events on at least 3 separate VPS boxes (v0 @ 37.60.251.252, v2 @ 34.58.165.14, v86 @ 192.150.253.122, per project memory). That's implausibly uniform for a code bug. Far more likely: all 6 validators are trying to accrue shares against a **shared on-chain invariant** (e.g., OutcomeVoting's signer set, or a share-accrual modifier introduced between v1275 and v1362) that is itself rejecting every submission. Alternative: a **shared off-chain dependency** (subtensor RPC, metagraph sync, a common chain RPC provider that's rate-limiting or returning stale state) is wedged and all 6 silently fall back to "no-op scoring" mode.
- **Diagnosis (ranked, network-wide lens):** (1) **Most likely — on-chain share-accrual path gated by a contract invariant that's rejecting submissions**: e.g., `OutcomeVoting.submitVote()` or whichever share-accrual method exists is reverting silently for every validator. Either the signer set is misconfigured, or a `Pausable`/`AccessControl`/`whenNotPaused` modifier is rejecting all calls post-some-event. Cheapest verification: `cast call 0xAD534f4CAB13707BD4d65e4EF086A455e6A643e5 "paused()(bool)" --rpc-url https://sepolia.base.org` plus a `cast logs` tail to look for failed tx patterns. (2) **Plausible — shared RPC endpoint degradation** making on-chain share-accrual tx submission fail across the fleet: v189 already shows `chain_connected:false` this iter; if the public `https://sepolia.base.org` endpoint is degraded for everyone but 5/6 validators haven't noticed because `/health.chain_connected` is a read-only probe, submission could silently fail while reads still succeed. (3) **Possible — shared metagraph-sync or stake-snapshot failure**: if Bittensor's subtensor sync has been stuck for the fleet, validators can't produce valid share-accrual inputs even if intake + settlement work. Weaker because `bt_connected:true` on all 6, but doesn't rule out a stale metagraph that returns stale data on fresh reads. (4) **ELIMINATED — per-validator operator lag** (iter-37 ruled this out for v0; iter-38 extends the ruling to the whole fleet across 3 restart events across 3 operators).
- **Pending-outcomes pattern:** v0 dropped 22 → 20 (2 outcomes settled without share accrual to UID 0 — see PASS entry for discussion). v1=8, v2=24, v86=6, v189=8, v213=15 at iter-38; iter-37 didn't capture these so no deltas yet. **Iter-39 deltas on every validator's pending_outcomes are load-bearing:** if pending decreases on MULTIPLE validators while shares stay flat fleet-wide, the settlement path works but share-accrual is independently broken (strongest form of diagnosis (1)). If pending grows or flatlines on ALL validators while shares are flat, the whole outcome pipeline is stuck (diagnosis (3) with stale metagraph OR diagnosis (2) with chain-write failure).
- **Repro:** 
  ```
  for h in v0 v1 v2 v86 v189 v213; do
    echo -n "$h: "
    curl -sS -m 10 "https://$h.djinn.gg/health" | jq -c '{v:.version, s:.shares_held, p:.pending_outcomes, cc:.chain_connected, sr:.settlement_registered}'
  done
  ```
  At 2026-04-20 00:52 UTC this returns 6 rows where `.s` (shares_held) matches iter-37's values (~44 min earlier) BYTE-IDENTICAL on every validator. Healthy drift rate from the pre-iter-33 baseline was ±3-7 shares per validator per hour.
- **Triage hint (escalated from iter-37's validator-state guidance to fleet-level action):**
  - **(1) First cheap diagnostic — on-chain invariant check via cast** (owned by /djinn, read-only, one-off):
    ```
    cast call 0xAD534f4CAB13707BD4d65e4EF086A455e6A643e5 "paused()(bool)" --rpc-url https://sepolia.base.org
    cast logs 0xAD534f4CAB13707BD4d65e4EF086A455e6A643e5 --from-block -5000 --rpc-url https://sepolia.base.org | tail -50
    ```
    If `paused()=true`, the OV contract has been paused (legitimately via timelock or accidentally) and no accruals can land — **fastest possible explanation for all 6 being flat simultaneously**. If `paused()=false` but recent event logs show failed Vote submissions, diagnosis (1) narrows to a specific revert reason. **Do this first before any other triage.**
  - **(2) Shared RPC probe**: send a trivial read tx (e.g., `eth_blockNumber`) to `https://sepolia.base.org` from each validator's hotkey. If submissions fail across the fleet, diagnosis (2). If only v189 fails (consistent with its `chain_connected:false`), diagnosis (1) + a single operator fix for v189.
  - **(3) Add regression test** (legit /djinn-e2e work): extend `e2e/ux/journeys/validator-health.spec.ts:47` to also assert `shares_held` moved by at least 1 across a two-call window for EACH validator (space the calls ~2 min apart; the suite already takes ~7m so budget is there). That would have caught this iter's stall in red, not in prose, AND would catch future fleet-wide accrual failures automatically. Patch sketch: add a second `/health` fetch 2 min after the first, delta-check with jitter tolerance of ±2 shares to avoid false positives.
  - **Do NOT** SSH, redeploy, restart, or otherwise touch validator infrastructure from /djinn-e2e. This is strictly an observer finding — /djinn owns remediation.
- **Severity rationale:** CRITICAL because (a) iter-37 CRITICAL explicitly pre-registered this escalation threshold — "network-wide = World-Cup-blocking" — and the condition is now met, (b) a 6-of-6 share-accrual halt across 5 code versions is exactly the pattern that CAN'T self-heal without external intervention (one stuck box heals on restart; 6 stuck boxes across version families don't), (c) **mainnet launch cannot proceed with a stalled emissions path** — the whole point of Bittensor + Djinn is that validator work → emissions, and if shares don't accrue, no emissions flow, no burn-address economics work, no incentives align, (d) **World Cup traffic spike (54 days)** would arrive at a network whose scoring is silently dead — user-visible symptoms would be absent until settlement timeouts start firing, then catastrophic, (e) MAINNET_BLOCKERS.md P0-01 is now **doubly broken** (UID 189 signer-registration per iter-37 HIGH + whole-fleet share stall per this CRITICAL) and the mainnet-launch clock should not advance until both heal. The HIGHEST-priority action for /djinn next is NOT a web/ code task — it's the `cast call` probe in triage hint (1) above.
- **Screenshot:** n/a (API-level; all 6 validator readings captured inline in PASS entry above).

### 2026-04-20 03:00 UTC — HIGH — `fetchFromAnyValidator` aborts its own winner's body stream; "The user aborted a request." renders on /network (Scoring Matrix) and /idiot/browse (signals list)

- [x] (fixed in v1386 per-probe AbortControllers; spec guards added in v1387 so the regression suite asserts neither "The user aborted a request." nor "Failed to load signals" leaks into /network or /idiot/browse DOM) Reported by `/djinn-e2e` heuristic sweep (iter-41, chrome channel + xvfb + saved `.vercel-session.json`). Two user-visible regressions from the v1380 parallel-race work (`5bef9847`). Regression suite DID NOT catch either — see coverage-gap note at the bottom.
- **Symptom:** A first-time visitor lands on two of the site's most load-bearing pages and sees a literal AbortError string rendered in the content area:
  - `/network` — the "Scoring Matrix" card (directly below the Miner Rank chart) shows the text "The user aborted a request." instead of the per-validator score heatmap. The card is inside `section.mb-8 > div.card.p-4.overflow-hidden > div.text-sm.text-slate-500.py-6` — the component's error branch.
  - `/idiot/browse` — after the filter/sort chrome renders, where the 4-card signal grid should be, the page shows "0 signals available / Failed to load signals / The user aborted a request." Grid is empty. First-time buyers cannot discover any signals on djinn.gg through the golden-path page.
- **Diagnosis:** single root cause in `web/lib/api.ts:357-392` (`fetchFromAnyValidator`). The function creates ONE `AbortController`, hands `controller.signal` to every probe fetch (`signal: controller.signal` at line 374), awaits `Promise.any(probes)` for the winner, then at line 382 calls `controller.abort(new Error("race_won"))` to cancel the losers. BUT the winner's `Response` body is a `ReadableStream` bound to the SAME signal — aborting the controller cancels the winner's body mid-stream. Downstream `await r.json()` in consumers then throws a DOMException whose `.message === "The user aborted a request."` (Chrome's canonical AbortError message). Two consumers catch it and render `e.message` verbatim into the DOM:
  1. `web/components/network/ScoringMatrix.tsx:217-226` — `.catch((e) => setError(e.message))` at line 224, then rendered at line 333: `{error || "Matrix data unavailable."}`.
  2. `web/lib/hooks/useBrowseSignals.ts:17-25` + `:53-58` — `fetchBrowseSignals` calls `fetchFromAnyValidator` at line 17, reads `res.json()` at line 25 (which throws); `useBrowseSignals` exposes `query.error.message` at line 55; the `/idiot/browse` page renders it as the "Failed to load signals / ..." block. The `0 signals available` text above it comes from the empty `signals` array when the query rejected.
  - The race IS working in one sense: `Promise.any` DOES resolve with the fastest host's response-headers-received moment. But because the body hasn't finished streaming when `controller.abort` fires ~microseconds later, `res.json()` sees a cancelled stream. In local dev this often doesn't repro because the body is small enough to have already flushed; in production through the nginx wildcard router the body arrives after the abort signal propagates.
  - Not a regression in the cache or the validator — `curl -sS https://v0.djinn.gg/v1/network/matrix | jq '.validators | length'` returns a valid number; `curl -sS 'https://v0.djinn.gg/v1/idiot/browse?limit=100'` returns a valid JSON. Pure client-side bug in the abort-on-race path.
- **Repro:**
  ```
  # Pass the Vercel challenge once (under xvfb-run), then:
  xvfb-run node -e "
    import('/home/user/djinn/web/node_modules/.pnpm/playwright@1.58.2/node_modules/playwright/index.mjs').then(async ({chromium}) => {
      const b = await chromium.launch({channel:'chrome', headless:false, args:['--disable-blink-features=AutomationControlled','--no-sandbox']});
      const ctx = await b.newContext({viewport:{width:1440,height:900}, storageState:'/home/user/djinn/web/e2e/ux/.vercel-session.json'});
      const p = await ctx.newPage();
      await p.goto('https://djinn.gg/idiot/browse');
      await p.waitForTimeout(15000);
      const t = await p.evaluate(() => document.body.innerText);
      console.log(t.includes('The user aborted a request') ? 'BUG REPROD' : 'clean');
      await b.close();
    });
  "
  ```
  Observe `BUG REPROD`. Same pattern at `/network`. Run in real Chrome on djinn.gg/idiot/browse and scroll — same bug, visible.
- **Triage hint:** fix is in `web/lib/api.ts:365-392`. Three options, in order of preference:
  1. **Don't abort on race-won at all** — remove line 382 (`controller.abort(new Error("race_won"))`). The loser connections will close naturally when GC'd; the short-lived extra sockets are not worth a production bug. Set `timeoutId` cleanup only. This is the smallest diff and matches how most "race for fastest" implementations work.
  2. **Per-probe AbortControllers, shared caller signal** — give each probe its own `AbortController`; on winner-picked, abort only the LOSERS by iterating them. Caller-signal cascade stays via `callerSignal.addEventListener("abort", ...)` on each probe controller. More lines, but explicit.
  3. **Fully buffer the winner** — `const body = await winner.arrayBuffer(); controller.abort("race_won"); return new Response(body, winner);`. Works but defeats streaming and doubles memory on large responses. Not recommended for `/v1/network/matrix` if it grows.
  - Tests to add (`web/lib/__tests__/api.test.ts`): a case where the winning host's body finishes AFTER the losers' headers would have arrived, asserting `await res.json()` still resolves. The current 3 vitest cases (`5bef9847`) only check `res.ok`, not body-read; that's why the regression didn't catch this.
  - `/network` MiningStats + NetworkStats + per-miner `[uid]/view.tsx` all call `fetchFromAnyValidator` too — audit those for the same pattern (they probably hit the bug any time the payload is more than a few KB). `web/app/error.tsx` and `web/components/ReportError.tsx` also call it (error-reporting use only; less critical).
- **Regression-coverage gap** (also fixable by /djinn-e2e + /djinn, not in this finding's scope): (a) `visitor.spec.ts:94-98` visits `/network` and asserts URL only — scoring-matrix content is never checked; (b) `idiot.spec.ts:146-190` tolerates `0` signal cards (`if (cardCount > 0) { ... }`), so "Failed to load signals" renders green-pass. Both journeys pass today while these error strings are user-visible. Fix sketch (`/djinn` follow-up): tighten `visitor.spec.ts` to assert the scoring matrix shows either a `<table>` or a specific empty-state string (NOT AbortError); tighten `idiot.spec.ts` to require either `signalCards.count > 0` OR a specific empty-state like "No signals available yet" (NOT "Failed to load signals"). Both changes together would have failed iter-41 in red and been caught before v1380 shipped to prod.
- **Severity rationale:** HIGH, not CRITICAL. (a) golden-path for a connected buyer is broken on `/idiot/browse` — this alone could justify CRITICAL, but (b) the full purchase flow via `purchase.spec.ts:70` still passes (?!) — meaning the signal-browse inside the authenticated dashboard uses a different code path OR retries masked the bug during the 1.3m test window; need to confirm which; (c) `/network` scoring matrix is a trust-surface (validators advertising their scoring), not a transactional blocker; (d) first-time-visitor conversion damage is real but the Nav + hero + About + Leaderboard + Docs + Attest all render fine, so the site isn't unusable — just ugly in two specific cards. With 54 days to the World Cup, HIGH is correct: fix before launch, not this hour. Escalate to CRITICAL if `purchase.spec.ts` stops passing (would indicate the authenticated browse path is also broken).
- **Screenshot:** `e2e-screenshots/network-fullpage-aborted-2026-04-20.png` (full `/network` page with "The user aborted a request." visible mid-page in Scoring Matrix card); `e2e-screenshots/network-scoring-matrix-aborted-2026-04-20.png` (viewport snapshot of Scoring Matrix section on desktop 1440×900).

### 2026-04-20 05:55 UTC — CRITICAL — v1402 "notional → bet size" revert INCOMPLETE: `/leaderboard` still renders "Bet size" in How Quality Score Works, breaks parameterized forbidden-phrase sweep (2 tests red, desktop + mobile)

- [x] (fixed in v1406 · commit `8e89bf99` · `web/app/leaderboard/page.tsx:244,247` "Bet size" → "Notional" in Quality Score Works block; zero occurrences remain site-wide per `grep -rniE '\bbet.?size\b' web/app web/components web/lib`) Reported by `/djinn-e2e` regression (parameterized.spec.ts:133 iter-1 desktop leaderboard + iter-1 mobile leaderboard). **2 of 74 tests failed** — the ONLY failures of iter-44. First iter-44 red, ending iter-42→43 clean streak at 2.

  - `Favorable: +Bet size × (odds − 1)` (line 244 of `web/app/leaderboard/page.tsx`)
  - `Unfavorable: −Bet size × Backing%` (line 247)
  Both render inside the "How Quality Score Works" explanatory block beneath the genius ranking table. Desktop and mobile viewports both fail identically.

The forbidden-phrase test was FLIPPED in the same commit (notional un-banned, bet-size banned), so this page was a ticking time bomb from the moment v1402 landed. This is exactly the failure mode flagged in `feedback_jargon_lockin_pattern.md` / commit message "every jargon rename must be pinned in parameterized.spec.ts FORBIDDEN_PHRASES with legal/docs carve-outs, or it silently regresses" — the lockin fired correctly, but the rename itself was incomplete.

No allowlist exception is correct here — leaderboard is a top-level public (indexed) page, explicitly the "legal positioning" context the commit was protecting. DEV-046 in DEVIATIONS.md already names `/education` as follow-up scope for residual "bet" language; leaderboard should be added to that list (or at minimum flagged here so `/djinn` grabs the same file in the same pass). Expect the fix to unblock both desktop + mobile failures in one shot (identical DOM assertion, two viewports). **Do NOT** add a leaderboard allowlist to ALLOWED_EXCEPTIONS — that would silently re-entrench the jargon lockin failure mode v1402 was meant to prevent.

- [x] (fixed in v1430) MED — `validator-health.spec.ts:47` FAIL: UID 1 direct-axon (`http://167.150.153.103:8421`) `socket hang up` on single /health probe during regression. Reported by `/djinn-e2e` regression. **Symptom:** `settlement-contract drift detected: UID 1: health fetch threw Error: apiRequestContext.get: socket hang up` (10s test-side timeout), only v1 axon affected; sibling test `validator-health.spec.ts:19 each known-good validator responds to /health` PASSED in the same run (uses `Promise.allSettled` — more forgiving than sequential loop). **Diagnosis:** General Tensor R's axon at `167.150.153.103:8421` (HTTP, no TLS) is transient-flaky. Manual `curl` reproduction 2 min after test ended: 4/5 OK (1.3-1.9s each) + 1/5 10s TIMEOUT — confirms ~20% flake rate on direct axon. HTTPS wildcard subdomain `v1.djinn.gg` was 3/3 fast (1.3-1.7s) at same moment, so the nginx+Caddy proxy layer is fine; issue is at the raw axon socket. Likely bittensor-default axon behavior (defaults to SSE+keepalive which can hang under load). **Repro:** `for i in 1 2 3 4 5; do curl -sS -m 10 -o /dev/null -w "try${i}: %{http_code}|%{time_total}s\n" http://167.150.153.103:8421/health; done` — expect ~1/5 TIMEOUT. **Triage hint:** (a) user-facing impact is LOW — real users hit `v1.djinn.gg` HTTPS proxy, which was fast and clean; (b) `validator-health.spec.ts` is an infrastructure health test, not a user journey; (c) options to fix test flakiness: (i) raise per-probe `timeout: 10000` → `timeout: 20000` (matches the one-slow-probe tolerance on v189), OR (ii) switch UID 1 host in `KNOWN_HEALTHY_UIDS` from `http://167.150.153.103:8421` → `https://v1.djinn.gg` to use the proxy path consistently with UID 0. Option (ii) is cleaner — UID 0 is already proxied; converting v1/v2/v86/v189/v213 to their `v<uid>.djinn.gg` subdomains (where they exist) would unify the test on the path real users actually take. **Screenshot:** n/a (endpoint-level).

### 2026-04-21 10:14 UTC — MED — `/network` miner Incentive column shows "0.00%" for all miners; chart Y-axis in unreadable scientific notation (`1.2e-6`)

- [x] (fixed in v1583) Reported by `/djinn-e2e` background (heuristic sweep iter-67). Fix: renamed `u16ToPercent` → `normalizedToPercent` and `formatStake` → `formatTao` in `web/components/network/helpers.ts`, corrected the math to match the real API shape (validator now returns normalized floats for incentive/validatorTrust and stringified-TAO for stake/emission — not the old rao/u16 units). Updated 4 call sites across `/network` page + validator detail view + `IncentiveChart`. Admin page keeps its own local u16/rao formatters untouched (different data path — sybil detector still uses raw bittensor units).
- **Symptom:** The Miners table on `/network` shows "0.00%" in the INCENTIVE column for every miner. The Miner Rank bar chart's Y-axis is labeled "Incentive %" but tick marks read `1.2000000E-6`, `1.0000000E-6`, `8.0000000E-7`, etc. (scientific notation sub-millionth range). Both are visually useless. The EMISSION column also shows "0.0000" for all rows.
- **Diagnosis:** `u16ToPercent()` in `web/components/network/helpers.ts:12` computes `(val / 65535) * 100`. This formula is correct for raw Bittensor u16 integers (range 0-65535), but the `/api/network/status` endpoint now forwards data directly from the validator's `/v1/network/overview`, which returns incentive as a **normalized float** (range 0.0-1.0, e.g. `0.000762` for top miners). Applying `/65535` to an already-normalized float gives `0.000762/65535*100 = 1.16e-6`, which rounds to 0.00%. The same bug affects the `validatorTrust` column for validators (also a normalized float from the validator API). `IncentiveChart.tsx`'s `getValue()` function at `web/components/network/IncentiveChart.tsx` has the same double-division: `return (m.incentive / 65535) * 100`, producing chart Y-axis values in the `1e-6` range instead of `0.01–0.1%`.
- **Repro:** Navigate to `https://djinn.gg/network`, scroll to the Miners table; observe INCENTIVE column = "0.00%" for all 246 rows. Observe chart Y-axis labels in scientific notation. Confirm via `curl -s http://37.60.251.252:8421/v1/network/overview | python3 -c "import sys,json; m=json.load(sys.stdin)['miners'][0]; print(m['uid'], m['incentive'])"` — returns a float like `0.000762`, not a u16 integer.
- **Triage hint:** Fix has two parts: (1) in `web/components/network/helpers.ts`, change `u16ToPercent` to `(val * 100).toFixed(4) + "%"` OR add a new helper `normalizedToPercent` for the float path and update callers. (2) in `web/components/network/IncentiveChart.tsx`, change `getValue` to return `m.incentive * 100` instead of `(m.incentive / 65535) * 100`. The EMISSION column uses `formatStake(m.emission)` where `m.emission` is `"0.114..."` (a float string), and `formatStake` in `helpers.ts:5` parses it as BigInt (rao) — check if that's the cause of "0.0000" emission display too. DO NOT change `u16ToPercent` if it's used anywhere that genuinely receives u16 integers — grep for all callers first (4 usages total in network pages).
- **Screenshot:** `e2e-screenshots/heuristic-network-table.png` (shows all "0.00%" in INCENTIVE column).

### 2026-04-22 04:30 UTC — MED — Leaderboard: no Basescan link for genius track record verification

- [x] (fixed in abd08686) Added `ExternalLink` icon next to each genius address on `/leaderboard` linking to `sepolia.basescan.org/address/{addr}`. Uses new `web/lib/explorer.ts::addressUrl` helper so a mainnet cutover flips via `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_EXPLORER` instead of per-file edits.
- **Symptom:** On `/leaderboard`, each genius row displays a truncated wallet address with a "Copy full address" button (`app/leaderboard/page.tsx:184-188`). There is no "View on Basescan" link. A first-time buyer who wants to independently verify the audit history (Quality Score, Win Rate, ROI) shown on the leaderboard must manually copy the address and paste it into `sepolia.basescan.org` — two steps that most users will not take.
- **Diagnosis:** The leaderboard currently trusts the API response for audit data and presents it as fact. No in-page link bridges the gap to on-chain proof. The contracts page (`/docs/contracts`) correctly links each proxy to Basescan, but the leaderboard (the primary trust signal for purchase decisions) does not.
- **Repro:** Navigate to `/leaderboard`. Click the copy icon next to any genius address. Observe: no "View on explorer" affordance anywhere on the row or in the expanded detail.
- **Triage hint:** Add an `<a href={\`https://sepolia.basescan.org/address/\${entry.address}\`} target="_blank">` icon button (e.g. `ExternalLink` icon from lucide-react) next to the copy button at `app/leaderboard/page.tsx:185`. For post-mainnet, swap `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_EXPLORER` env var. The admin page already defines this pattern at `app/admin/page.tsx:197`. For World Cup traffic, the trust gap matters: buyers will look for on-chain proof before spending USDC.

### 2026-04-22 04:30 UTC — MED — Purchase flow: txHash captured but never shown to buyer

- [x] (fixed in abd08686) When `txHash` is set during the purchase flow, the progress panel renders a Basescan link `{txHash[:10]}…{txHash[-8:]}  View on Basescan` so the buyer can watch their tx confirm in real time. Uses new `web/lib/explorer.ts::txUrl` helper.
- **Symptom:** After a successful purchase, the buyer has no way to see their on-chain transaction hash in the UI. The `txHash` is captured in state (`app/idiot/signal/page.tsx:233`) and used only to gate a loading state transition (`purchaseLoading && !txHash`), but is never rendered. A buyer who wants to confirm their payment on Basescan must find the transaction in their wallet app — an extra step that creates anxiety ("did it actually go through?").
- **Diagnosis:** The `usePurchase` hook returns `txHash` after the on-chain transaction confirms. The purchase page reads it but discards it visually. There is no "Transaction confirmed — View on Basescan" step in the purchase flow.
- **Repro:** Complete a signal purchase as an idiot. After the wallet confirmation, observe the UI transitions to the share-collection step with no transaction link visible at any point.
- **Triage hint:** After the on-chain step completes (state transitions away from `purchasing_chain`), render a brief `<a href={\`${BASE_EXPLORER}/tx/\${txHash}\`} target="_blank">View transaction</a>` alongside the share-collection progress. The `BASE_EXPLORER` pattern is already in `app/admin/mission-control.tsx:31` — reuse it. Fix is approximately 5 lines in the purchase success branch of the signal page.

The validator's ort=85 (still resolving locally) but cannot submit OV votes while chain_connected=False. OV quorum threshold is 4; with UID 189 offline for chain writes, the margin is exactly 1 (UIDs 0/1/2/86/213 can vote). If UID 213 (v999, intermittently restarting) also goes down simultaneously, quorum is unreachable.
- **Diagnosis:** UID 189's Base Sepolia RPC endpoint appears to go down periodically. The validator reconnects (chain=True) then loses connectivity again. Prior observations: iter-70 (chain=False first detected), iter-71 (chain=True), iter-73 (chain=True → marked resolved), iter-73b (chain=False again). The underlying issue is the operator's RPC endpoint reliability, not our code.
- **Repro:** Probe `curl -s http://161.97.150.248:8421/health | python3 -c "import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin).get('chain_connected'))"` repeatedly. Expect intermittent `False` responses.
- **Triage hint:** The iter-73 "AUTO-RESOLVED" entry was incorrect; this MED finding supersedes it. OV quorum is still reachable (5 validators when UID 189 is disconnected), so settlement is not immediately blocked. But if any second validator loses chain connectivity simultaneously, settlement stalls. Operator action: UID 189's operator should check their Base Sepolia RPC endpoint (either Alchemy/Infura key hitting rate limits, or a provider outage). The `chain_connected` flag is set in `validator/server.py` when the EVM provider fails health check. Consider switching to a different RPC provider or adding a fallback URL (BACKUP_BASE_RPC_URL feature if not already present).

### 2026-04-18 — MED — Purchase journey: test clicks disabled "Unavailable" button when signal MPC keys still distributing

- [x] (fixed in this commit — added isEnabled() poll loop 4×15s before click; falls through to existing purchase-blocked branch if still disabled)
- [x] (this line is the correction entry itself, not a defect — fix landed in b29f5af; marking closed so the triage count stays honest) Discovered immediately after filing the iter-9 INFO entry — that entry had claimed "9 consecutive clean runs" based on 4 cached prior outputs; the actual just-completed UI run (`bs2iol2yd`) was **7 passed, 1 failed**. Correcting now.
- **Failed test:** `e2e/ux/journeys/purchase.spec.ts:274` (`Purchase Journey › genius creates signal, idiot deposits and browses`). Other 7 journeys all passed; total run 6.5m.
- **Failure point:** `await purchaseBtn.click()` at line 274 timed out.
- **Root cause (product state — real, not a defect):** The signal detail page shows the alert "Signal Temporarily Unavailable / Validators are still distributing encryption key shares for this signal. This page re-checks automatically every 15 seconds." The purchase CTA renders as `<button>Unavailable</button>` with `[disabled]`. This is correct product behavior immediately after signal creation while MPC key-share distribution is in flight.
- **Test defect:** Selector `getByRole("button", { name: /purchase signal|purchase|buy/i })` finds the disabled button (likely matches via aria-label "Purchase Signal" on the disabled CTA), `isVisible()` returns true, the test enters the `if (canPurchase)` block, types notional, then `click()` times out waiting for the disabled button to become clickable. The test never checks `isEnabled()` and never special-cases the documented "Signal Temporarily Unavailable" alert.
- **Suggested fix** (not for `/djinn-e2e` to apply directly — observer-only):
  - Before clicking, assert `await purchaseBtn.isEnabled()`. If false, capture the alert text (which the page literally tells us — "still distributing encryption key shares") and either (a) wait + re-check up to N times, or (b) annotate as `purchase-deferred` and exit cleanly. The current pattern at line 308 (`purchase-blocked`) already exists for similar paths — extend it.
  - Alternatively, the purchase journey could wait longer between Phase 1 (genius creates signal) and Phase 2 (idiot purchases) so MPC distribution has time to complete. Today the timing is back-to-back; bumping to 60–90s gap would likely flip this from intermittent failure to consistent pass.
- **Why this matters:** This is the only journey that exercises the actual product money path (signal → purchase). When it false-fails on a known transient state, every iter-N regression sweep gets noisier and real regressions get harder to spot.
- **Repro:** `cd ~/djinn/web && set -a && source .env.local && set +a && xvfb-run npx playwright test --config playwright.ux.config.ts --grep purchase`. Trace at `web/test-results/purchase-Purchase-Journey--5316e--idiot-deposits-and-browses-ux-journeys/trace.zip`. Re-run consistency: failed 1× out of 5 recent runs (4× pass per iters 5–8, 1× fail iter 9), so it's intermittent — depends on whether MPC share distribution completes within the 15s purchase-button-visible window.
- **Net for iter-9 INFO entry above:** the "9 consecutive clean runs" line should be read as "8 of 9 runs clean; 1 intermittent test-side flake on the most fragile journey." Other 7 journeys remain rock-solid (visitor / genius / idiot / resilience×4 / nothing-burger).

  - **Misc** — `/v1/burn/verify`, `/v1/preferences/{address}`, `/v1/signals/resolve`, `/v1/telemetry`

### 2026-04-18 — HIGH — Validator settlement-contract drift across the fleet (4 of 6 wrong)

- [x] (fixed in 648c46a + operator upgrades to v1243+) Discovered by `/loop /djinn-e2e` iter 5 fleet-direct API audit (extending UID 0 check from iter 4 to all known UIDs). Resolved by /loop iter 8 re-audit: all 6 reachable validators now ✓. Root-cause fix (`648c46a` validator: ignore OUTCOME_VOTING_ADDRESS env override by default) plus operators upgrading to v1243+ flipped UID 1/86/189 from ✗ to ✓; UID 213 (still v999) also now ✓ — likely operator updated .env per the embedded `settlement_remediation` text.
- **What I observed** (curl `http://<ip>:8421/health` for each UID, expected `settlement_contract == 0xAD534f4CAB13707BD4d65e4EF086A455e6A643e5` — the live OutcomeVoting proxy):

  | UID | IP | version | settlement_registered | settlement_contract | verdict |
  |-----|----|---------|----------------------|---------------------|---------|
  | 0   | 37.60.251.252  | v1234+2 | True  | 0xAD534f4CAB… | ✅ correct |
  | 1   | 167.150.153.103 | v1235  | **False** | 0x28b5738ff3… | ❌ wrong addr A |
  | 2   | 34.58.165.14    | v1234  | True  | 0xAD534f4CAB… | ✅ correct |
  | 86  | 192.150.253.122 | v1236  | **False** | 0x4b140aA4Bf… | ❌ wrong addr B |
  | 189 | 161.97.150.248  | v1235  | **False** | 0x28b5738ff3… | ❌ wrong addr A |
  | 201 | 5.161.74.227    | —      | —     | —              | ⚪ offline (matches memory) |
  | 213 | 3.150.72.96     | v999   | **True** | **0x4b140aA4Bf…** | ⚠️ registered, but to wrong addr B |
- **Why this matters:** Only **2 of 6 reachable validators** are pointed at the correct OutcomeVoting proxy. UID 213's `settlement_registered: True` against a wrong address is the most concerning case — it thinks it's settling correctly. This degrades settlement consensus in proportion to the fleet share that's misconfigured (right now ~67% of the reachable fleet is voting on the wrong contract or not at all).
- **Two distinct stale addresses observed**:
  - `0x28b5738ff3…` on UIDs 1 & 189 (likely an older OutcomeVoting from a pre-launch deployment)
  - `0x4b140aA4Bf…` on UIDs 86 & 213 (different older deploy)
- **Relationship to existing memory:** Extends `project_outcomevoting_stale_address_2026_04_17.md` — that memo predicted UIDs 1/86/189 had stale `OUTCOME_VOTING_ADDRESS` in `.env`. New data shows: (a) the issue persists ~24h later, (b) UID 213 is also affected and worse (registered=True against wrong addr), (c) two separate stale addresses are in the wild, suggesting at least two prior deployments are still in operator `.env` files.
- **Suggested operator action** (not for `/djinn` to apply directly — needs operator-by-operator outreach):
  1. Each affected operator runs `cast call <their .env OUTCOME_VOTING_ADDRESS> "owner()(address)"` and confirms whether it returns the live Timelock `0x37f41E…`. If not, they're on a dead deploy.
  2. Update `.env` to `OUTCOME_VOTING_ADDRESS=0xAD534f4CAB13707BD4d65e4EF086A455e6A643e5`, restart validator, confirm `/health.settlement_contract` matches and `settlement_registered: True`.
  3. UID 213's "registered=True against wrong addr" needs the most urgent attention — its votes are being recorded on a contract that nobody else is reading.
- **Note for the website surface:** Worth verifying whether the `/network` UI page surfaces this mismatch to operators (does it show each validator's `settlement_contract`?). If not, that's a useful UX add — operators currently have no in-product signal that they're misconfigured.
- **UI suite (iter 5):** 8/8 PASS, 6.2m. No regressions.
- **Repro:** `for ip in 37.60.251.252 167.150.153.103 34.58.165.14 192.150.253.122 161.97.150.248 3.150.72.96; do curl -s --max-time 6 http://$ip:8421/health | jq '{uid,version,settlement_registered,settlement_contract}'; done`

One anonymous-read test: navigate Home → /attest via top-nav, assert "Web Attestation" H1 + 3-step "How it works" list + URL input with `https://httpbin.org/get` placeholder + Attest button disabled-when-empty / enabled-when-typed, then submit `http://example.com` and assert the client-side `URL must start with https://` guard fires without calling `/api/attest` (verified via `page.route` spy). Deliberately avoids an actual TLSNotary round-trip: those take 30s–3min depending on miner availability and introduce flake that isn't the regression-sweep's job to absorb. Proveaudit server round-trip is a separate cousin-project test; if we want end-to-end, that lands as `attest-proveaudit.spec.ts` in a follow-up when proveaudit's CI hook is ready.
- **Why this lives in UX_FINDINGS.md:** `/djinn-e2e` is explicitly observer-only (never commits product code). Expanding test coverage is product code. The /djinn improvement loop is the right place to implement the specs above; this entry is the queue.

### 2026-04-18 — MED — JSON parse error during rapid client-side navigation

- [x] (fixed in 535452f) Reported by `/djinn-e2e` resilience suite (`web/e2e/ux/journeys/resilience.spec.ts:37` — "survive rapid navigation without crashing").
- **Symptom:** During a two-pass rapid loop through Genius → Idiot → Leaderboard → Network → Docs → About → Home (14 navigations with 300-1500ms gaps), one uncaught page error fires:
  ```
  Unexpected token '<', "<!DOCTYPE "... is not valid JSON
  ```
- **Diagnosis:** A client-side `fetch().json()` received an HTML payload (likely `<!DOCTYPE html>...`) instead of JSON. Suspects in order of likelihood:
  1. A `/api/*` route that returned an HTML error page (Vercel 404, edge-runtime crash, or auth gate) for an in-flight request when the user had already navigated away.
  2. The Vercel Security Checkpoint serving its HTML challenge to a background fetch (the test harness logged "Vercel Security Checkpoint detected, waiting for resolution..." earlier in the run).
  3. A stale CDN cache returning the SPA shell for what was meant to be a JSON endpoint.
- **Repro:** Run `/djinn-e2e` (or just `xvfb-run npx playwright test --config playwright.ux.config.ts --grep resilience`). Failure consumed the whole resilience suite — the next 3 sub-tests (`wallet persists`, `page refresh`, `mobile viewport`) did not run.
- **Triage hint:** The resilience filter already ignores `Warning:`, `favicon`, `walletconnect`, hydration, ChunkLoadError, ResizeObserver, abort, and React #418/422/423/425. So this is a fetch the codebase didn't anticipate aborting cleanly. Likely fix lives in whichever data-fetch hook drives one of the 7 nav targets — wrap `r.json()` in a content-type check, or have the API route never return HTML.
- **Other 4 journeys (visitor / genius / idiot / purchase) all PASSED** in the same run — the golden path is healthy.
